Stable Liftings of Polynomial Traces on Tetrahedra Thanks: Submitted to the editors DATE.
Charles Parker, Endre Süli

Abstract
On the reference tetrahedron K K , we construct, for each k ∈ ℕ 0 k\in\mathbb{N}_{0} , a right inverse for the trace operator u ↦ ( u , ∂ 𝐧 u , … , ∂ 𝐧 k u ) | ∂ K u\mapsto(u,\partial_{\mathbf{n}}u,\ldots,\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{k}u)|_{\partial K} . The operator is stable as a mapping from the trace space of W s , p ​ ( K ) W^{s,p}(K) to W s , p ​ ( K ) W^{s,p}(K) for all p ∈ ( 1 , ∞ ) p\in(1,\infty) and s ∈ ( k + 1 / p , ∞ ) s\in(k+1/p,\infty) . Moreover, if the data is the trace of a polynomial of degree N ∈ ℕ 0 N\in\mathbb{N}_{0} , then the resulting lifting is a polynomial of degree N N . One consequence of the analysis is a novel characterization for the range of the trace operator.



keywords

trace lifting, polynomial extension, polynomial lifting

Funding.
The first author acknowledges that this material is based upon work supported by the National Science Foundation under Award No.
DMS-2201487.

runningheads:
Stable Liftings of Polynomial Traces on Tetrahedra / C. Parker and E. Süli

AMS

46E35, 65N30

1
Introduction

The numerical analysis of high-order finite element and spectral element methods heavily rely on the existence of stable polynomial liftings – bounded operators mapping suitable piecewise polynomials on the boundary of the element to polynomials defined over the entire element. A number of operators have been constructed on the reference triangle and square, beginning with the pioneering work of Babuška et al.
[
13
,
14
]
. Their lifting maps
H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial E)
boundedly into
H^{1}(E)
, where
E
is either a suitable reference triangle or square, with the additional property that if the datum is continuous and its restriction to each edge is a polynomial of degree
N\geq 0
, then the lifting is a polynomial of degree
N
. Other constructions for continuous piecewise polynomials on
\partial E
are stable from a discrete trace space to
L^{2}(E)

[
3
]
, from
L^{2}(\partial E)
to
H^{\frac{1}{2}}(E)

[
2
]
, from
W^{1-\frac{1}{p},p}(\partial E)
to
W^{1,p}(E)
for
1<p<\infty

[
44
]
, and from
W^{s-\frac{1}{p},p}(\partial E)
to
W^{s,p}(E)
for
s\geq 1
and
1<p<\infty

[
47
]
. Liftings for other types of traces are also available; e.g. lifting the normal trace of
H(\mathrm{div};E)

[
2
]
, lifting the trace and normal derivative simultaneously into
H^{2}(E)

[
5
]
, and lifting an arbitrary number of normal derivatives simultaneously into
W^{s,p}(E)

[
47
]
.

Many of the above results have been extended to three space dimensions. Muñoz-Sola
[
45
]
generalized the construction of Babuška et al.
[
13
,
14
]
to the tetrahedron, while Belgacem
[
15
]
gave a different construction for the cube using orthogonal polynomials. Commuting lifting operators for the spaces appearing in the de Rham complex on tetrahedra
[
29
,
30
,
31
]
and hexahedra
[
26
]
have also been constructed. These operators, among others, have been used extensively in a priori error analysis
[
7
,
14
,
35
,
38
,
44
,
45
]
, a posteriori error analysis
[
21
,
24
,
25
,
36
]
, the analysis of preconditioners
[
3
,
4
,
8
,
9
,
10
,
13
,
48
]
, the analysis of sprectral element methods, particularly in weighted Sobolev spaces
[
17
,
18
,
19
,
20
]
, and in the stability analysis of mixed finite element methods
[
6
,
12
,
27
,
28
,
32
,
42
]
. Nevertheless, two types of operators are notably missing from the currently available results in three dimensions: (i) lifting operators stable in
L^{p}
-based Sobelev spaces, crucial in the analysis of high-order finite element methods for nonlinear problems; and (ii) lifting operators for the simultaneous lifting of the trace and normal derivative (and higher-order normal derivatives) which appear in the analysis of fourth-order (and higher-order) problems and in the analysis of mixed finite element methods for problems in electromagnetism and incompressible flow.

We address both of the above problems; namely, for each
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we construct a right inverse for the trace operator
u\mapsto(u,\partial_{\mathbf{n}}u,\ldots,\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{k}u)|_{\partial K}
on the reference tetrahedron
K
that is stable from the trace of
W^{s,p}(K)
to
W^{s,p}(K)
for all
p\in(1,\infty)
and
s\in(k+1/p,\infty)
. Additionally, if the data is the trace of a polynomial of degree
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then the resulting lifting is a polynomial of degree
N
. A precise statement appears at the end of
section

2
, which also contains a characterization for the trace space that appears to be novel. These results generalize our construction on the reference triangle
[
47
]
to the reference tetrahedron and to Sobolev spaces with minimal regularity.

The remainder of the manuscript is organized as follows. In
section

3
, we detail an explicit construction of the lifting operator in a sequence of four steps, each consisting of an intermediate single-face lifting operator. The remainder of the manuscript is devoted to the analysis of the intermediate single-face operators:
sections

4
and
5
characterize the continuity of a related operator defined on all of
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, while
section

6
concludes with the proofs of the continuity properties of the intermediate operators.

2
The traces of
W^{s,q}(K)
functions and statement of main result

We begin by reviewing the regularity properties of the traces of a function
u
defined on a tetrahedron. Here, we will work in the setting of Sobolev spaces defined on an open Lipschitz domain
\mathcal{O}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Let
s=m+\sigma
be a nonnegative real number with
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\sigma\in[0,1)
. We denote by
W^{s,p}(\mathcal{O})
,
p\in[1,\infty)
, the standard fractional Sobolev(-Slobodeckij) space
[
1
]
equipped with norm defined by

\displaystyle\|v\|_{s,p,\mathcal{O}}^{p}:=\sum_{n=1}^{m}|v|_{n,p,\mathcal{O}}^{p}+\begin{cases}\sum_{|\alpha|=m}|D^{\alpha}v|_{\sigma,p,\mathcal{O}}^{p}&\text{if }\sigma>0,\\
0&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

where the integer-valued seminorms and fractional seminorms are given by

\displaystyle|v|_{n,p,\mathcal{O}}^{p}:=\sum_{|\alpha|=n}\int_{\mathcal{O}}|D^{\alpha}v(\bm{x})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\quad\text{and}\quad|v|_{\sigma,p,\mathcal{O}}^{p}:=\iint_{\mathcal{O}\times\mathcal{O}}\frac{|v(\bm{x})-v(\bm{y})|^{p}}{|\bm{x}-\bm{y}|^{\sigma p+d}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}},

with the usual modification for
p=\infty
. When
s=0
, the Sobolev space
W^{0,p}(\mathcal{O})
coincides with the standard Lebesque space
L^{p}(\mathcal{O})
, and we denote the norm by
\|\cdot\|_{p,\mathcal{O}}
. We also require fractional Sobolev spaces defined on domain boundaries. Given a
C^{k,1}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
(d-1)
-dimensional manifold
\Gamma\subseteq\partial\mathcal{O}
, the surface gradient
D_{\Gamma}
is well-defined a.e. on
\Gamma
, and we define
W^{s,p}(\Gamma)
,
0\leq s\leq k+1
, analogously (see e.g.
[
46
, §2.5.2]
) with the norm

\displaystyle\|v\|_{s,p,\Gamma}^{p}:=\sum_{|\beta|\leq m}\int_{\Gamma}|D_{\Gamma}^{\beta}v(\bm{x})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}+\sum_{|\beta|=m}\iint_{\Gamma\times\Gamma}\frac{|D_{\Gamma}^{\beta}v(\bm{x})-D_{\Gamma}^{\beta}v(\bm{y})|^{p}}{|\bm{x}-\bm{y}|^{\sigma p+d-1}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}},

where the sums are over multi-indices
\beta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d-1}
. The seminorms
|\cdot|_{s,p,\Gamma}
are defined similarly.

2.1
Elementary trace results

\bm{v}_{1}=\mathbf{e}_{3}
\bm{v}_{2}=\mathbf{e}_{2}
\bm{v}_{3}=\mathbf{e}_{1}
\bm{v}_{4}=\mathbf{0}
{\gamma}_{12}
{\gamma}_{13}
{\gamma}_{14}
{\gamma}_{23}
{\gamma}_{24}
{\gamma}_{34}
\Gamma_{1}
\Gamma_{2}
\Gamma_{3}

(a)

\bm{a}_{3}=\mathbf{0}
\bm{a}_{1}=\mathbf{e}_{1}
\bm{a}_{2}=\mathbf{e}_{2}
{\gamma}_{3}
{\gamma}_{1}
{\gamma}_{2}

(b)

Figure 1
:
Reference (a) tetrahedron and (b) triangle, where
\mathbf{e}_{i}
are the standard unit vectors. Note that the label for
\Gamma_{4}=\{(x,y,z)\in\bar{K}:x+y+z=1\}
is omitted in (a).

When the domain is the reference tetrahedron
K:=\{(x,y,z)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:0<x,y,z,x+y+z<1\}
depicted in
fig.

1(a)
, the space
W^{r,p}(\partial K)
,
0\leq r<1
, may be equipped with an equivalent norm that is more amenable to the analysis of traces. Let
\Gamma_{i}
and
\Gamma_{j}
,
1\leq i<j\leq 4
, be two faces of
K
and let
\gamma_{ij}=\gamma_{ji}
denote the shared edge with vertices
\bm{a}
and
\bm{b}
. Then, the vertices of
\Gamma_{i}
are denoted by
\bm{a}
,
\bm{b}
, and
\bm{c}_{i}
, while the vertices of
\Gamma_{j}
are denoted by
\bm{a}
,
\bm{b}
, and
\bm{c}_{j}
. Since
\Gamma_{i}
and
\Gamma_{j}
are both triangles, there exist unique affine mappings
\bm{F}_{ij}:T\to\Gamma_{i}
and
\bm{F}_{ji}:T\to\Gamma_{j}
from the reference triangle
T:=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:0<x,y,x+y<1\}
, labeled as in
fig.

1(b)
, satisfying

\displaystyle\bm{F}_{ij}(0,0)

\displaystyle=\bm{a},\qquad

\displaystyle\bm{F}_{ij}(1,0)=\bm{b},\quad\text{and}\quad

\displaystyle\bm{F}_{ij}(0,1)

\displaystyle=\bm{c}_{i},

(1a)

\displaystyle\bm{F}_{ji}(0,0)

\displaystyle=\bm{a},\qquad

\displaystyle\bm{F}_{ji}(1,0)=\bm{b},\quad\text{and}\quad

\displaystyle\bm{F}_{ji}(0,1)

\displaystyle=\bm{c}_{j},

(1b)

and we define the following norm:

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{r,p,\partial K}^{p}:=\sum_{i=1}^{4}\|f_{i}\|_{r,p,\Gamma_{i}}^{p}+\begin{cases}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 4}\mathcal{I}_{ij}^{p}(f_{i},f_{j})&\text{if }rp=1,\\
0&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

where
f_{i}
denotes the restriction of
f
to
\Gamma_{i}
and
\mathcal{I}_{ij}^{p}(f,g)
is defined by the rule

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{ij}^{p}(f,g):=\int_{T}|f\circ\bm{F}_{ij}(\bm{x})-g\circ\bm{F}_{ji}(\bm{x})|^{p}\frac{\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}}{x_{2}}.

(2)

Thanks to
corollary

44
,
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{r,p,\partial K}
is an equivalent norm on
W^{r,p}(\partial K)
; i.e.

\displaystyle\|f\|_{r,p,\partial K}\approx_{r,p}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{r,p,\partial K}\qquad\forall f\in W^{r,p}(\partial K),

(3)

and we shall use the two norms interchangeably with the common notation
\|\cdot\|_{r,p,K}
. Here, and in what follows, we use the standard notation
a\lesssim_{c}b
to indicate
a\leq Cb
where
C
is a constant depending only on
c
, and
a\approx_{c}b
if
a\lesssim_{c}b
and
b\lesssim_{c}a
.

Now let
u\in W^{s,p}(K)
,
1<p<\infty
,
s=m+\sigma>1/p
with
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\sigma\in[0,1)
, be a function defined on the reference tetrahedron. The presence of edges and corners on the boundary of
K
limit the regularity of the trace of
u
. Nevertheless, we can iteratively apply the standard
W^{s,p}(K)
trace theorem (e.g.
[
40
, Theorem 3.1]
or
[
41
, p. 208 Theorem 1]
):
W^{s,p}(K)
embeds continuously into
W^{s-\frac{1}{p},p}(\partial K)
for
1/p<s<1+1/p
. In particular, for
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, the
k
th-order derivative tensor given by

\displaystyle(D^{k}u)_{i_{1}i_{2}\ldots i_{k}}=\partial_{x_{i_{1}}}\partial_{x_{i_{2}}}\cdots\partial_{x_{i_{k}}}u

satisfies
D^{k}u\in W^{s-k,p}(K)\subset W^{1+\sigma,p}(K)
,
0\leq k\leq m-1
, and
D^{m}u\in W^{\sigma,p}(K)
; thus, the traces satisfy

\displaystyle\begin{cases}D^{k}u|_{\partial K}\in W^{1-\frac{1}{p},p}(\partial K)&\text{for }0\leq k<s-\frac{1}{p},\\
D^{m-1}u|_{\partial K}\in W^{1+\sigma-\frac{1}{p},p}(\partial K)&\text{if }m\geq 1\text{ and }\sigma p<1,\\
D^{m}u|_{\partial K}\in W^{\sigma-\frac{1}{p},p}(\partial K)&\text{if }\sigma p>1.\end{cases}

Additionally, the trace of
W^{m+\frac{1}{2},2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
,
m\geq 1
, on the plane
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\{0\}
belongs to
W^{m,2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
(see e.g.
[
1
, Chapter 7]
or
[
41
, p. 20 Theorem 4]
), and so standard arguments show that the trace of
W^{m+\frac{1}{2},2}(K)
on the face
\Gamma_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq 4
, belongs to
W^{m,2}(\Gamma_{i})
. Thanks to the norm-equivalence
eq.

3
, we arrive at the following conditions:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \sum_{i=1}^{4}\|D^{k}u\|_{p,\Gamma_{i}}^{p}&<\infty\quad&&\text{for }0\leq k<s-\frac{1}{p},\\
\sum_{i=1}^{4}\|D^{m-1}u\|_{1+\sigma-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{i}}^{p}&<\infty\quad&&\text{if }m\geq 1\text{ and either }\sigma p<1\text{ or }(\sigma,p)=\left(\frac{1}{2},2\right),\\
\sum_{i=1}^{4}\|D^{m}u\|_{\sigma-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{i}}^{p}&<\infty\quad&&\text{if }\sigma p>1,\\
\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 4}\mathcal{I}_{ij}^{p}(D^{m}u,D^{m}u)&<\infty\quad&&\text{if }\sigma p=2.\end{aligned}\right.

(4)

Remark 1
.

The case
\sigma p=1
for
p\neq 2
, which is not included in conditions
eq.

4
, is beyond the scope of this paper since the trace of a
W^{m+\frac{1}{p},p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
, function on the plane
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\{0\}
belongs to a Besov space, which cannot be identified with an integer-order Sobolev space
[
41
, p. 20 Theorem 4]
.

When
s>2/p
, we obtain additional conditions since the trace of a
W^{s,p}(K)
function on the edge
\gamma_{ij}
,
1\leq i<j\leq 4
is well-defined. This can be seen from standard arguments owing to the fact that the trace of
W^{s,p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
on the line
\mathbb{R}\times\{0\}^{2}
is well-defined. In particular, the traces of the
k
-th derivative tensor,
0\leq k<s-2/p
, on
\Gamma_{i}
and
\Gamma_{j}
,
1\leq i<j\leq 4
, must agree on the shared edge
\gamma_{ij}
:

\displaystyle D^{k}u|_{\Gamma_{i}}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=D^{k}u|_{\Gamma_{j}}(\bm{x})\qquad\text{for a.e. $\bm{x}\in\gamma_{ij}$ and all }0\leq k<s-\frac{2}{p},

(5)

where
eq.

5
is to be interpreted in the trace sense.

2.2
Trace operators

We now turn to the consequences of
eqs.

4
and
5
for various trace operators.

2.2.1
Zeroth-order operator

First consider the the
0
th-order “boundary-derivative” operator
\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{0}
on
\Gamma_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq 4
, defined formally by the rule

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{0}(f):=f\qquad\text{on }\Gamma_{i}.

(6)

Then,
eqs.

4
and
5
show that for
u\in W^{s,p}(K)
,
(s,q)\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{k}:=\left\{(s,p)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:1<p<\infty,\ (s-k)p>1,\text{ and }s-\frac{1}{p}\notin\mathbb{Z}\text{ if }p\neq 2\right\},\quad k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},

(7)

the trace
f=u|_{\partial K}
satisfies the following conditions:

1.

W^{s-\frac{1}{p},p}
regularity on each face:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{0}(f)\in W^{s-\frac{1}{p},p}(\Gamma_{i}),\qquad 1\leq i\leq 4.

(8)

2.

Compatible traces along edges: For
1\leq i<j\leq 4
, there holds

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{0}(f)|_{\gamma_{ij}}-\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{0}(f)|_{\gamma_{ij}}

\displaystyle=0\qquad

\displaystyle\text{if }sp>2,

(9a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{ij}^{p}(\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{0}(f),\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{0}(f))

\displaystyle<\infty\qquad

\displaystyle\text{if }sp=2.

(9b)

If
(s-n)p=2
for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
, then we obtain an additional condition since the
n
-th derivative tensor satisfies
\mathcal{I}_{ij}^{p}(D^{n}u,D^{n}u)<\infty
for
1\leq i<j\leq 4
. To describe this condition we define the following notation for a
d
-dimensional tensor
S
and vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
:

\displaystyle v^{\otimes 0}\cdot S:=S\quad\text{and}\quad v^{\otimes j}\cdot S:=S_{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{d}}v_{i_{1}}v_{i_{2}}\cdots v_{i_{j}},\qquad 1\leq j\leq d.

In particular, for
1\leq i<j\leq 4
, denoting by
\mathbf{t}_{ij}
a unit vector tangent to
\gamma_{ij}
, we can differentiate
\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{0}(u)
and
\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{0}(u)
in the direction
\mathbf{t}_{ij}
to obtain the following identity.

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{n}\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{0}(u)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{n}}=\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\otimes n}\cdot D^{n}u|_{\Gamma_{i}}\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\partial^{n}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{0}(u)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{n}}=\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\otimes n}\cdot D^{n}u|_{\Gamma_{j}}.

Consequently, the trace
f=u|_{\partial K}
also satisfies the following property:

3.

Compatible tangential derivatives: For
1\leq i<j\leq 4
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, there holds

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{ij}^{p}\left(\frac{\partial^{n}\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{0}(u)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{n}},\frac{\partial^{n}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{0}(u)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{n}}\right)<\infty\qquad\text{if }(s-n)p=2.

(10)

2.2.2
First-order operator

For
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{1}
, we turn to the regularity of the trace of the gradient of
u\in W^{s,p}(K)
. To this end, on each face
\Gamma_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq 4
, let
\{\bm{\tau}_{i,1},\bm{\tau}_{i,2}\}
be orthonormal vectors tangent to
\Gamma_{i}
and let
\mathbf{n}_{i}
denote the outward unit normal vector on
\Gamma_{i}
. We define the 1st-order “boundary-derivative” operator
\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{1}
on
\Gamma_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq 4
, by the rule

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{1}(f,g):=\sum_{j=1}^{2}\frac{\partial f}{\partial\bm{\tau}_{i,j}}\bm{\tau}_{i,j}+g\mathbf{n}_{i}\qquad\text{on }\Gamma_{i},

(11)

so that
\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{1}(u,\partial_{\mathbf{n}}u)=Du|_{\Gamma_{i}}
. Again applying
eqs.

4
and
5
, we obtain analogues of
eqs.

8
and
9
stated below in
eqs.

13
and
14
with
k=0
. However, if
(s-2)p>2
, then the second derivative tensor has matching traces on edges (i.e.
eq.

5
holds with
k=2
). In particular, for
1\leq i<j\leq 4
, we define the vectors

\displaystyle\mathbf{b}_{ij}:=\mathbf{t}_{ij}\times\mathbf{n}_{i}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbf{b}_{ji}:=\mathbf{t}_{ij}\times\mathbf{n}_{j},

(12)

where we recall that
\mathbf{t}_{ij}
is a unit vector tangent to
\gamma_{ij}
, so that on
\gamma_{ij}
, there holds

\displaystyle\mathbf{b}_{ji}\cdot\frac{\partial\mathfrak{D}^{1}_{i}(u,\partial_{\mathbf{n}}u)}{\partial\mathbf{b}_{ij}}=\mathbf{b}_{ji}\cdot\frac{\partial Du}{\partial\mathbf{b}_{ij}}=\frac{\partial^{2}u}{\partial\mathbf{b}_{ij}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}}=\mathbf{b}_{ij}\cdot\frac{\partial Du}{\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}}=\mathbf{b}_{ij}\cdot\frac{\partial\mathfrak{D}^{1}_{j}(u,\partial_{\mathbf{n}}u)}{\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}}

in the sense of traces. As a consequence, the operator
\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{1}
satisfies the additional condition
eq.

15
below with
n=0
thanks to
eqs.

4
and
5
. Finally, we can differentiate in the direction tangent to each edge to obtain the analogue of
eq.

10
stated in
eqs.

14b
and
15b
below. To summarize, the traces
f=u|_{\partial K}
and
g=\partial_{\mathbf{n}}u|_{\partial K}
satisfy the following for all
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{1}
:

1.

W^{s-1-\frac{1}{p},p}
regularity on each face:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{1}(f,g)\in W^{s-1-\frac{1}{p},p}(\Gamma_{i}),\qquad 1\leq i\leq 4.

(13)

2.

Compatible traces along edges: For
1\leq i<j\leq 4
and
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, there holds

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{1}(f,g)|_{\gamma_{ij}}-\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{1}(f,g)|_{\gamma_{ij}}

\displaystyle=0\qquad

\displaystyle\text{if }(s-1)p>2,

(14a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{ij}^{p}\left(\frac{\partial^{n}\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{1}(f,g)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{n}},\frac{\partial^{n}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{1}(f,g)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{n}}\right)

\displaystyle<\infty\qquad

\displaystyle\text{if }(s-n-1)p=2.

(14b)

3.

Compatible traces of higher derivatives along edges: For
1\leq i<j\leq 4
and
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, there holds

\displaystyle\left.\mathbf{b}_{ji}\cdot\frac{\partial\mathfrak{D}^{1}_{i}(f,g)}{\partial\mathbf{b}_{ij}}\right|_{\gamma_{ij}}-\left.\mathbf{b}_{ij}\cdot\frac{\partial\mathfrak{D}^{1}_{j}(f,g)}{\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}}\right|_{\gamma_{ij}}

\displaystyle=0\qquad

\displaystyle\text{if }(s-2)p>2,

(15a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{ij}^{p}\left(\mathbf{b}_{ji}\cdot\frac{\partial^{n+1}\mathfrak{D}^{1}_{i}(f,g)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{n}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ij}},\mathbf{b}_{ij}\cdot\frac{\partial^{n+1}\mathfrak{D}^{1}_{j}(f,g)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{n}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}}\right)

\displaystyle<\infty\qquad

\displaystyle\text{if }(s-n-2)p=2.

(15b)

Remark 2
.

For smooth enough functions, conditions
eqs.

14a
and
15a

may be interpreted as the application of the vertex compatibility conditions for traces on the triangle (see e.g.
[
47
, eqs. (2.11a) and (2.12a)]
) at every point on the edge
\gamma_{ij}
.

2.2.3
m
th-order operator

We now turn to the general case of the trace of the
m
-th derivative tensor of a function
u\in W^{s,p}(K)
, where
m\geq 2
and
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{m}
. Given a collection of functions
F=(f^{0},f^{1},\ldots,f^{m})
defined on
\partial K
, we define the
m
-th order “boundary-derivative” operator
\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{m}
on
\Gamma_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq 4
, by the rule

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{m}(F):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{3}\\
|\alpha|=m\end{subarray}}\frac{\partial^{\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}}f^{\alpha_{3}}}{\partial\bm{\tau}_{i,1}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial\bm{\tau}_{i,2}^{\alpha_{2}}}\sum_{\phi\in\mathfrak{M}_{i}(\alpha)}\phi(1)\otimes\phi(2)\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi(m)\qquad\text{on }\Gamma_{i},

(16)

where the set
\mathfrak{M}_{i}(\alpha)
consists of the following mappings.

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{i}(\alpha):=\{\phi:\{1,2,\ldots,|\alpha|\}\to\{\bm{\tau}_{i,1},\bm{\tau}_{i,2},\mathbf{n}_{i}\}\text{ s.t. }|\phi^{-1}(\bm{\tau}_{i,j})|=\alpha_{j},\ j=1,2\},

where we recall that
\{\bm{\tau}_{i,1},\bm{\tau}_{i,2}\}
are orthonormal vectors tangent to
\Gamma_{i}
. For notational convenience, we set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{l}(F):=\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{l}(f^{0},f^{1},\ldots,f^{l}),\qquad 0\leq l<m.

Then, one may readily verify that
\mathfrak{D}^{m}_{i}(u,\partial_{\mathbf{n}}u,\ldots,\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}u)=D^{m}u
on
\Gamma_{i}
. Let
f^{l}=\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{l}u
on
\partial K
. As before, we obtain
W^{s-m-\frac{1}{p},p}
regularity of
\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{m}(F)

eq.

17
below on each face thanks to
eq.

4
and the edge compatibility conditions
eq.

18
below with
l=0
from
eq.

5
.

As was the case with the first-order operator, there are additional edge compatibility conditions. In particular, if
(s-m-l)p>0
for some
1\leq l\leq m
, then
eq.

5
shows that the
(m+l)
th derivative tensor has matching traces on edges. Some components of the
(m+l)
th derivative tensor can be expressed in terms of
\mathfrak{D}_{l}^{m}(F)
. In particular, on the edge
\gamma_{ij}
,
1\leq i<j\leq 4
, there holds

\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\otimes l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{l}\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{m}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{l}}=\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\otimes l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{l}D^{m}u}{\partial\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{l}}=\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\otimes l}\cdot\left(\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{\otimes l}\cdot D^{m+l}u\right)\\
=\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{\otimes l}\cdot\left(\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\otimes l}\cdot D^{m+l}u\right)=\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{\otimes l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{l}D^{m}u}{\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{l}}=\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{\otimes l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{l}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{m}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{l}}

in the sense of traces, where we used symmetry of the derivative tensor
D^{m+l}u
. We can also differentiate in the direction tangent to each edge to obtain similar conditions. Consequently,
\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{m}(F)
satisfies
eq.

18
below. In summary, for
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, the traces
F=(u,\partial_{\mathbf{n}}u,\ldots,\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}u)
satisfy the following for all
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{m}
:

1.

W^{s-m-\frac{1}{p},p}
regularity on each face:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{m}(F)\in W^{s-m-\frac{1}{p},p}(\Gamma_{i}),\qquad 1\leq i\leq 4,

(17)

where
\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{0}
,
\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{1}
, and
\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{l}
,
l\geq 2
, are defined in
eq.

6
,
eq.

11
, and
eq.

16
.

2.

Compatible traces along edges: For
1\leq i<j\leq 4
and
0\leq l\leq m
and
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, there holds

\displaystyle\left.\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\otimes l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{l}\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{m}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{l}}\right|_{\gamma_{ij}}-\left.\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{\otimes l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{l}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{m}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{l}}\right|_{\gamma_{ij}}

\displaystyle=0\qquad

\displaystyle\text{if }(s-m-l)p>2,

(18a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{ij}^{p}\left(\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\otimes l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{l+n}\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{m}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{n}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{l}},\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{\otimes l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{l+n}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{m}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{n}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{l}}\right)

\displaystyle<\infty\qquad

\displaystyle\text{if }(s-m-l-n)p=2.

(18b)

Remark 3
.

As was the case in
remark

2
, condition
eq.

18a
is simply the application of the vertex compatibility conditions for traces on the triangle
[
47
, eq. (7.2)]
at every point on the edge
\gamma_{ij}
, provided that
u
is smooth enough.

2.3
The trace theorem on a tetrahedron

Motivated by the conditions derived in the previous section, we define trace spaces as follows. Given a set of indices
\mathcal{S}\subseteq\{1,2,3,4\}
with
|\mathcal{S}|\geq 1
, let
\Gamma_{S}:=\cup_{i\in\mathcal{S}}\Gamma_{i}
. We define the trace space on part of the boundary
\Tr_{k}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}})
for
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
as follows.

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Tr^{s,p}_{k}(\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}})&:=\{F=(f^{0},f^{1},\ldots,f^{k})\in L^{p}(\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}})^{k+1}:\text{For $0\leq m\leq k$,}\\
&\qquad\quad\text{$F$ satisfies \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:review:sigmak-face-reg} for $i\in\mathcal{S}$ and }\\
&\qquad\quad\text{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:review:sigmak-cont-combo} for $i,j\in\mathcal{S}$ with $i<j$, $0\leq l\leq m$ and $n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}$}\},\end{aligned}

equipped with the norm

\|(f^{0},f^{1},\ldots,f^{k})\|_{\Tr_{k}^{s,p},\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}}^{p}:=\sum_{m=0}^{k}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{S}}\|f_{i}^{m}\|_{s-m-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{i}}^{p}\\
+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\in\mathcal{S}\\
i<j\\
0\leq l\leq k\\
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\end{subarray}}\begin{cases}\mathcal{I}_{ij}^{p}\left(\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\otimes l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{l+n}\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{k}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{n}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{l}},\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{\otimes l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{l+n}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{k}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{n}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{l}}\right)&\text{if }(s-k-l-n)p=2,\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Note that the sum in the definition contains only finitely many nonzero terms, and hence is well defined. When
\mathcal{S}=\{1,2,3,4\}
, we set
\Tr^{s,p}_{k}(\partial K):=\Tr^{s,p}_{k}(\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}})
and
\|\cdot\|_{\Tr_{k}^{s,p},\partial K}:=\|\cdot\|_{\Tr_{k}^{s,p},\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}}
. The following trace theorem is a consequence of the discussion in the previous section.

Theorem 4
.

Let
\mathcal{S}\subseteq\{1,2,3,4\}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, and
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
be given. Then, for every
u\in W^{s,p}(K)
, the traces satisfy
(u,\partial_{\mathbf{n}}u,\ldots,\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{k}u)|_{\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}}\in\Tr_{k}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}})
and

\displaystyle\|(u,\partial_{\mathbf{n}}u,\ldots,\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{k}u)\|_{\Tr_{k}^{s,p},\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}}\lesssim_{k,s,p}\|u\|_{s,p,K}.

(19)

2.4
The trace of polynomials

Given
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, let
\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
denote the set of all polynomials of total degree at most
N
, while
\mathcal{P}_{-M}:=\{0\}
for
M>0
. If
u\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
, then
u\in W^{s,p}(K)
for all
s\geq 0
and
p\geq 1
. Consequently, for each
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\mathcal{S}\subseteq\{1,2,3,4\}
, the traces
F=(u,\partial_{\mathbf{n}}u,\ldots,\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{k}u)|_{\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}}\in\Tr^{s,p}_{k}(\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}})
for all
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
. In particular,
s
may be taken to be arbitrarily large in
eq.

18a
. Thus, the traces satisfy

\displaystyle f^{m}_{i}

\displaystyle\in\mathcal{P}_{N-m}(\Gamma_{i}),\qquad

\displaystyle 0\leq m\leq k,\ i\in\mathcal{S},

(20a)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{m}(F)|_{\gamma_{ij}}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{m}(F)|_{\gamma_{ij}},\qquad

\displaystyle 0\leq m\leq k,\ i,j\in\mathcal{S},\ i<j,

(20b)

\displaystyle\left.\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\otimes l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{l}\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{k}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{l}}\right|_{\gamma_{ij}}

\displaystyle=\left.\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{\otimes l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{l}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{k}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{l}}\right|_{\gamma_{ij}},\qquad

\displaystyle 0\leq l\leq k,\ i,j\in\mathcal{S},\ i<j.

(20c)

Note that we have not included the integral condition
eq.

18b
in the list
eq.

20
above. The following lemma shows that if a tuple of functions defined on
\partial K
satisfy
eq.

20
, then
eq.

18b
is automatically satisfied.

Lemma 5
.

Let
\mathcal{S}\subseteq\{1,2,3,4\}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. If
F:\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}\to\mathbb{R}^{k+1}
satisfies
eq.

20
, then
F\in\Tr_{k}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}})
for all
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
.

Proof.

Let
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
,
0\leq l\leq m\leq k
, be given. Thanks to
eqs.

20b
and
20c
, the difference

\displaystyle H_{ij}:=\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\otimes l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{l}\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{m}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{l}}\circ\bm{F}_{ij}-\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{\otimes l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{l}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{m}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{l}}\circ\bm{F}_{ji},\qquad\text{ on $T$},i,j\in\mathcal{S},

vanishes on the edge
\gamma_{2}
of the reference triangle
T
and
H_{ij}
has entries
\mathcal{P}_{N-m-l}(T)
. Thus,
H_{ij}=x_{2}G_{ij}
, where
G_{ij}
has entries in
\mathcal{P}_{N-m-l-1}(T)
. Consequently, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, there holds

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{ij}^{p}\left(\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\otimes l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{l+n}\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{m}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{n}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{l}},\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{\otimes l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{l+n}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{m}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{n}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{l}}\right)

\displaystyle\approx_{p}\int_{T}|\partial_{x_{1}}^{n}H_{ij}(\bm{x})|^{p}\frac{\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}}{x_{2}}

\displaystyle=\int_{T}|\partial_{x_{1}}^{n}G_{ij}(\bm{x})|^{p}x_{2}^{p-1}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}},

which is finite since
G_{ij}
has polynomial entries. The inclusion
F\in\Tr_{k}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}})
now follows from
eq.

20
.

2.5
Statement of the main result

The aim of the current work is to construct a right inverse
\mathcal{L}_{k}
of the operator
u\mapsto(u,\partial_{\mathbf{n}}u,\ldots,\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{k}u)|_{\partial K}
for each
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
that is bounded from
\Tr_{k}^{s,p}(\partial K)
into
W^{s,p}(K)
for all
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}

and
preserves polynomials in the following sense: if
F=(f^{0},f^{1},\ldots,f^{k})
is the trace of some degree
N
polynomial, then
\mathcal{L}_{k}(F)
is a polynomial of degree
N
. In particular, the main result is as follows.

Theorem 6
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. There exists a linear operator

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{k}:\bigcup_{(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}}\Tr_{k}^{s,p}(\partial K)\to L^{1}(K)

satisfying the following properties: for all
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
and
F=(f^{0},f^{1},\ldots,f^{k})\in\Tr_{k}^{s,p}(\partial K)
,
\mathcal{L}_{k}(F)\in W^{s,p}(K)
,

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{l}\mathcal{L}_{k}(F)|_{\partial K}=f^{l},\qquad 0\leq l\leq k,\quad\text{and}\quad\|\mathcal{L}_{k}(F)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{k,s,p}\|F\|_{\Tr_{k}^{s,p},\partial K}.

Moreover, if
F
is a piecewise polynomial of degree
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
satisfying
eq.

20
with
\mathcal{S}=\{1,2,3,4\}
, then
\mathcal{L}_{k}(F)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
.

The construction of the lifting operator
\mathcal{L}_{k}
in
theorem

6
is the focus of the next section, and the proof of
theorem

6
appears in
section

3.5
. An immediate consequence is the following characterization of the range of the trace operator.

Corollary 7
.

For each
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, the operator
u\mapsto(u,\partial_{\mathbf{n}}u,\ldots,\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{k}u)|_{\partial K}
is surjective from
W^{s,p}(K)
onto
\Tr_{k}^{s,p}(\partial K)
for all
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
.

3
Construction of the lifting operator

The construction of the lifting operator
\mathcal{L}_{k}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, proceeds face-by-face using similar techniques to
[
45
,
47
]
. The main idea is to perform a sequence of liftings and corrections using a fundamental convolution operator (see e.g.
[
11
, eq. (4.2)]
,
[
13
]
,
[
17
]
,
[
18
, p. 56, eq. (2.1)]
,
[
46
, §2.5.5]
) and subsequent modifications to it. Given a nonnegative integer
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, a smooth compactly supported function
b\in C_{c}^{\infty}(T)
, and a function
f:T\to\mathbb{R}
, we define the operator
\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}
formally by the rule

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}(f)(\bm{x},z):=\frac{(-z)^{k}}{k!}\int_{T}b(\bm{y})f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}\qquad\forall(\bm{x},z)\in K,

(21)

and we use the notation
\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}[b]
when we want to make the dependence on
b
explicit. Note that for
(\bm{x},z)\in K
and
\bm{y}\in T
, there holds
\bm{x}+z\bm{y}\in T
, and so
eq.

21
is well-defined for e.g.
f\in C^{\infty}(\bar{T})
. For functions
f:\Gamma_{1}\to\mathbb{R}
we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}(f):=\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}(f\circ\mathfrak{I}_{1}),\qquad\text{where}\quad\mathfrak{I}_{1}(\bm{x}):=(\bm{x},0)\qquad\forall\bm{x}\in T.

(22)

3.1
Lifting from one face

The first result concerns the interpolation and continuity properties of
\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}
.

Lemma 8
.

Let
b\in C_{c}^{\infty}(T)
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, and
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
. Then, for all
f\in W^{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p}(\Gamma_{1})
, there holds

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}(f)|_{\Gamma_{1}}=\delta_{mk}\left(\int_{T}b(\bm{x})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\right)f,\qquad 0\leq m\leq k,

(23)

and

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}(f)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,s,p}\|f\|_{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{1}}.

(24)

Moreover, if
f\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(\Gamma_{1})
,
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then
\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}(f)\in\mathcal{P}_{N+k}(K)
.

The proof appears in
section

6.1
. We now construct a lifting operator from
\Gamma_{1}
.

Lemma 9
.

Let
b\in C_{c}^{\infty}(T)
with
\int_{T}b(\bm{x})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}=1
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. We formally define the following operators for
F=(f^{0},f^{1},\ldots,f^{k})~\in~L^{p}(\Gamma_{1})^{k+1}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{0}^{[1]}(F)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{E}_{0}^{[1]}(f^{0}),\qquad

(25a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{m}^{[1]}(F)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{E}_{m}^{[1]}(f^{m}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\mathcal{L}_{m-1}^{[1]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{1}}),\qquad

\displaystyle 1\leq m\leq k.

(25b)

Then, for all
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
and
F\in\Tr_{k}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{1})
,
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}(F)
is well-defined and there holds

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{1}}=f^{m},\qquad 0\leq m\leq k,\quad\text{and}\quad\|\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,s,p}\|F\|_{\Tr_{k}^{s,p},\Gamma_{1}}.

(26)

Moreover, if
f^{m}\in\mathcal{P}_{N-m}(\Gamma_{1})
,
0\leq m\leq k
, for some
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}(F)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
k
. The case
k=0
follows immediately from
lemma

8
. Now assume that the lemma is true for some
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and let
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k+1}
and
F\in\Tr_{k+1}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{1})
be as in the statement of the lemma. Then, we may apply the lemma to
\tilde{F}=(f^{0},f^{1},\ldots,f^{k})\in\Tr_{k}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{1})
to conclude that for
0\leq m\leq k
and

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}(\tilde{F})|_{\Gamma_{1}}=f^{m},\ \ 0\leq m\leq k,\ \ \text{and}\ \ \|\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}(\tilde{F})\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,s,p}\|\tilde{F}\|_{\Tr_{k}^{s,p},\Gamma_{1}}.

Thanks to the trace theorem (
theorem

4
), there holds
f^{k+1}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{k+1}\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{1}}\in W^{s-k-1-\frac{1}{p},p}(\Gamma_{1})
with

\displaystyle\|f^{k+1}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{k+1}\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}(\tilde{F})\|_{s-k-1-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{b,k,s,p}\|F\|_{\Tr_{k+1}^{s,p},\Gamma_{1}},

and so
eq.

26
follows from
lemma

8
. Additionally, if
F
satisfies
f^{m}\in\mathcal{P}_{N-m}(\Gamma_{1})
,
0\leq m\leq k+1
, for some
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then
\tilde{F}
satisfies the same condition, where the upper bound of
m
is restricted to
k
. Consequently,
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}(\tilde{F})\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
and so
f^{k+1}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{k+1}\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}(\tilde{F})|_{\Gamma_{1}}\in\mathcal{P}_{N-k-1}(\Gamma_{1})
. Thus,
\mathcal{L}_{k+1}^{[1]}(F)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
thanks to
lemma

8
.

3.2
Lifting from two faces

We now seek a lifting operator from
\Gamma_{2}
that has zero trace on
\Gamma_{1}
. The operator will be a generalization of the form introduced in
[
45
]
. We first define an operator that lifts traces from
\Gamma_{1}
and has zero trace on
\Gamma_{2}
, and then define the lifting operator from
\Gamma_{2}
in terms of this operator. To this end, denote by
\omega_{i}
the barycentric coordinates of
T
extended to all of
\mathbb{R}^{2}
as follows.

\displaystyle\omega_{i}(\bm{x}):=\min\{x_{i},1\},\quad 1\leq i\leq 2,\quad\text{and}\quad\omega_{3}(\bm{x}):=1-\min\{x_{1}+x_{2},1\}\quad\forall\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}.

(27)

Given nonnegative integers
k,r\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, a smooth compactly supported function
b\in C_{c}^{\infty}(T)
, and a function
f:T\to\mathbb{R}
, we define the operator
\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[1]}
formally by the rule

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f)(\bm{x},z)&:=x_{2}^{r}\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}(\omega_{2}^{-r}f)(\bm{x},z)\\
&=x_{2}^{r}\frac{(-z)^{k}}{k!}\int_{T}\frac{b(\bm{y})f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})}{(x_{2}+zy_{2})^{r}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}\qquad\forall(\bm{x},z)\in K.\end{aligned}

(28)

Note that when
r=0
, we have
\mathcal{M}_{k,0}^{[1]}=\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}
. For functions
f:\Gamma_{1}\to\mathbb{R}
, we again abuse notation and set
\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f):=\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f\circ\mathfrak{I}_{1})
.

The presence of the weight
\omega_{2}^{-r}
in the operator
\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[1]}
means that derivatives of
f:\Gamma_{1}\to\mathbb{R}
up to order
r
have to vanish on edge
\gamma_{12}
in an appropriate sense. To this end, let
s=m+\sigma
with
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\sigma\in[0,1)
and
1<p<\infty
. Given a face
\Gamma_{j}
,
1\leq j\leq 4
, and
\mathfrak{E}
a subset of the edges of
\Gamma_{j}
, we define the following subspaces of
W^{s,p}(\Gamma_{j})
with vanishing traces on the edges in
\mathfrak{E}
:

W^{s,p}_{\mathfrak{E}}(\Gamma_{j}):=\left\{f\in W^{s,p}(\Gamma_{j}):D_{\Gamma}^{\beta}f|_{\gamma}=0\text{ for all }0\leq|\beta|<s-\frac{1}{p}\text{ and }\gamma\in\mathfrak{E}\right.\\
\left.\vphantom{\frac{1}{p}}\text{ and }\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-16.32437pt{\mathfrak{E}}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,T}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-16.32437pt{\mathfrak{E}}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,T}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-7.4375pt\kern 7.82639pt{}}}_{{\kern-10.31944pt{\mathfrak{E}}\kern 7.82639pt{s,p,T}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-5.2014pt\kern 5.59029pt{}}}_{{\kern-8.08334pt{\mathfrak{E}}\kern 5.59029pt{s,p,T}}}}<\infty\right\},

(29)

where the norm on
W^{s,p}_{\mathfrak{E}}(\Gamma_{j})
is given by

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{p}}}_{{\kern-16.32437pt{\mathfrak{E}}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{p}}}_{{\kern-16.32437pt{\mathfrak{E}}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-7.4375pt\kern 7.82639pt{p}}}_{{\kern-10.31944pt{\mathfrak{E}}\kern 7.82639pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-5.2014pt\kern 5.59029pt{p}}}_{{\kern-8.08334pt{\mathfrak{E}}\kern 5.59029pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}:=\|f\|_{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}^{p}+\begin{cases}\|\dist(\cdot,\bigcup_{\gamma\in\mathfrak{E}}\gamma)^{-\sigma}D_{\Gamma}^{m}f\|_{p,\Gamma_{j}}^{p}&\text{if }\sigma p=1\text{ and }\mathfrak{E}\neq\emptyset,\\
0&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

and we recall that
D_{\Gamma}
is the surface gradient operator.
When
\mathfrak{E}
consists of only one element
\gamma
, we set
W_{\gamma}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{j}):=W_{\mathfrak{E}}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{j})
and
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-15.52652pt{\gamma}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-15.52652pt{\gamma}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-7.4375pt\kern 7.82639pt{}}}_{{\kern-9.74956pt{\gamma}\kern 7.82639pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-5.2014pt\kern 5.59029pt{}}}_{{\kern-7.51346pt{\gamma}\kern 5.59029pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}:=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-16.32437pt{\mathfrak{E}}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-16.32437pt{\mathfrak{E}}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-7.4375pt\kern 7.82639pt{}}}_{{\kern-10.31944pt{\mathfrak{E}}\kern 7.82639pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-5.2014pt\kern 5.59029pt{}}}_{{\kern-8.08334pt{\mathfrak{E}}\kern 5.59029pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}
. One can readily verify that the spaces
W_{\mathfrak{E}}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{j})
are Banach spaces and that the following relations hold:

\displaystyle W_{\mathfrak{E}}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{j})=\bigcap_{\gamma\in\mathfrak{E}}W_{\gamma}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{j})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-16.32437pt{\mathfrak{E}}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-16.32437pt{\mathfrak{E}}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-7.4375pt\kern 7.82639pt{}}}_{{\kern-10.31944pt{\mathfrak{E}}\kern 7.82639pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-5.2014pt\kern 5.59029pt{}}}_{{\kern-8.08334pt{\mathfrak{E}}\kern 5.59029pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}\approx_{s,p}\sum_{\gamma\in\mathfrak{E}}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-15.52652pt{\gamma}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-15.52652pt{\gamma}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-7.4375pt\kern 7.82639pt{}}}_{{\kern-9.74956pt{\gamma}\kern 7.82639pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-5.2014pt\kern 5.59029pt{}}}_{{\kern-7.51346pt{\gamma}\kern 5.59029pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}.

(30)

Given a subset of edges
\mathfrak{E}
of the reference triangle
T
, we define the spaces
W_{\mathfrak{E}}^{s,p}(T)
analogously.

The first result states the continuity properties of
\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[1]}
.

Lemma 10
.

Let
b\in C_{c}^{\infty}(T)
,
k,r\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, and
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
. Then, for all

f~\in~W^{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p}(\Gamma_{1})\cap W_{\gamma_{12}}^{\min\{s-k-\frac{1}{p},r\},p}(\Gamma_{1})
, there holds

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f)|_{\Gamma_{1}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{km}\left(\int_{T}b(\bm{x})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\right)f,\qquad

\displaystyle 0\leq m\leq k,

(31a)

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{j}\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f)|_{\Gamma_{2}}

\displaystyle=0,\qquad

\displaystyle 0\leq j<\min\left\{r,s-\frac{1}{p}\right\},

(31b)

and

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,r,s,p}\begin{cases}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{12}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-19.52652pt{\gamma_{12}}\kern 12.87854pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{1}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{12}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-19.52652pt{\gamma_{12}}\kern 12.87854pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{1}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{12}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-7.4375pt\kern 7.82639pt{}}}_{{\kern-13.74956pt{\gamma_{12}}\kern 7.82639pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{1}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{12}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-5.2014pt\kern 5.59029pt{}}}_{{\kern-11.51346pt{\gamma_{12}}\kern 5.59029pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{1}}}}}&\text{if }s\leq k+r+\frac{1}{p},\\
\|f\|_{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{1}}&\text{if }s>k+r+\frac{1}{p}.\end{cases}

(32)

Moreover, if
f\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(\Gamma_{1})
,
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, satisfies
D_{\Gamma}^{l}f|_{\gamma_{12}}=0
for
0\leq l\leq r-1
, then
\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f)\in\mathcal{P}_{N+k}(K)
.

The proof of
lemma

10
appears in
section

6.3
.
By mapping the other faces of
K
to
\Gamma_{1}
and mapping
K
onto itself in an appropriate fashion, we may define operators corresponding to these faces. In particular, we define the following operator corresponding to
\Gamma_{2}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[2]}(f)(\bm{x},z):=\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f\circ\mathfrak{I}_{2})\circ\mathfrak{R}_{12}(\bm{x},z)\qquad\forall(\bm{x},z)\in K,

where
\mathfrak{I}_{2}(\bm{x}):=(x_{1},0,x_{2})
and
\mathfrak{R}_{12}(\bm{x},z):=(x_{1},z,x_{2})
for all
(\bm{x},z)\in K
.

Thanks to the chain rule and the smoothness of the mappings
\mathfrak{I}_{2}
and
\mathfrak{R}_{12}
, the continuity and interpolation properties of
\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[2]}
follow immediately from
lemma

10
.

Corollary 11
.

Let
b\in C_{c}^{\infty}(T)
,
k,r\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, and
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
. Then, for all
f\in W^{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p}(\Gamma_{2})\cap W_{\gamma_{12}}^{\min\{s-k-\frac{1}{p},r\},p}(\Gamma_{2})
, there holds

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[2]}(f)|_{\Gamma_{2}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{km}\left(\int_{T}b(\bm{x})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\right)f,\qquad

\displaystyle 0\leq m\leq k,

(33a)

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{j}\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[2]}(f)|_{\Gamma_{1}}

\displaystyle=0,\qquad

\displaystyle 0\leq j<\min\left\{r,s-\frac{1}{p}\right\},

(33b)

and

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[2]}(f)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,r,s,p}\begin{cases}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{12}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-19.52652pt{\gamma_{12}}\kern 12.87854pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{2}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{12}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-19.52652pt{\gamma_{12}}\kern 12.87854pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{2}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{12}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-7.4375pt\kern 7.82639pt{}}}_{{\kern-13.74956pt{\gamma_{12}}\kern 7.82639pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{2}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{12}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-5.2014pt\kern 5.59029pt{}}}_{{\kern-11.51346pt{\gamma_{12}}\kern 5.59029pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{2}}}}}&\text{if }s\leq k+r+\frac{1}{p},\\
\|f\|_{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{2}}&\text{if }s>k+r+\frac{1}{p}.\end{cases}

(34)

Moreover, if
f\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(\Gamma_{2})
,
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, satisfies
D_{\Gamma}^{l}f|_{\gamma_{12}}=0
for
0\leq l\leq r-1
, then
\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[2]}(f)\in\mathcal{P}_{N+k}(K)
.

3.2.1
Regularity of partially vanishing traces

The operator
\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[2]}
lifts traces from
\Gamma_{2}
to
K
and has zero trace on
\Gamma_{1}
, which are the properties we desired to correct the traces of
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}
on
\Gamma_{2}
. However,
\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[2]}
acts on functions belonging to
W^{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p}(\Gamma_{2})\cap W_{\gamma_{12}}^{\min\{s-k-\frac{1}{p},r\},p}(\Gamma_{2})
rather than just functions in
W^{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p}(\Gamma_{2})
. The main result of this section characterizes one scenario in which traces belong to the space
W^{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p}(\Gamma_{2})\cap W_{\gamma_{12}}^{\min\{s-k-\frac{1}{p},r\},p}(\Gamma_{2})
, and fortunately, we will encounter exactly this scenario in our construction.

We have the following result which characterizes the regularity of the restriction of a trace
F\in\Tr_{k}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{i}\cup\Gamma_{j})
to
\Gamma_{j}
when
F
vanishes on
\Gamma_{i}
and the first
l
components of
F
vanish on
\Gamma_{j}
.

Lemma 12
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
,
1\leq l\leq k
, and
1\leq i,j\leq 4
with
i\neq j
be given. Suppose that
F=(f^{0},f^{1},\ldots,f^{k})\in\Tr_{k}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{i}\cup\Gamma_{j})
satisfies

(i)

F=(0,0,\ldots,0)
on
\Gamma_{i}
;

(ii)

f_{j}^{m}=0
on
\Gamma_{j}
for
0\leq m\leq l-1
.

Then, there holds
f^{l}_{j}\in W^{s-l-\frac{1}{p},p}(\Gamma_{j})\cap W^{\min\{s-l-\frac{1}{p},k+1\},p}_{\gamma_{ij}}(\Gamma_{j})
and

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{ij}}}}}\left\|f_{j}^{l}\right\|^{{\kern-15.288pt\kern 15.67688pt{}}}_{{\kern-21.67232pt{\gamma_{ij}}\kern 15.67688pt{s-l-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{ij}}}}}\left\|f_{j}^{l}\right\|^{{\kern-15.288pt\kern 15.67688pt{}}}_{{\kern-21.67232pt{\gamma_{ij}}\kern 15.67688pt{s-l-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{ij}}}}}\left\|f_{j}^{l}\right\|^{{\kern-9.57916pt\kern 9.96805pt{}}}_{{\kern-15.23868pt{\gamma_{ij}}\kern 9.96805pt{s-l-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{ij}}}}}\left\|f_{j}^{l}\right\|^{{\kern-7.34306pt\kern 7.73195pt{}}}_{{\kern-13.00258pt{\gamma_{ij}}\kern 7.73195pt{s-l-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{k,s,p}\|F\|_{\Tr_{k}^{s,p},\Gamma_{i}\cup\Gamma_{j}}\qquad

\displaystyle\text{if }s-l\leq k+1+\frac{1}{p},

(35a)

\displaystyle\|f_{j}^{l}\|_{s-l-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{k,s,p}\|F\|_{\Tr_{k}^{s,p},\Gamma_{i}\cup\Gamma_{j}}\qquad

\displaystyle\text{if }s-l>k+1+\frac{1}{p}.

(35b)

Proof.

Without loss of generality, assume that
i<j
. We first show that for
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{2}
there holds

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial^{|\alpha|}f^{l}_{j}}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\alpha_{2}}}\right|_{\gamma_{ij}}\equiv 0\qquad 0\leq|\alpha|<\min\left\{s-l-\frac{2}{p},k+1\right\},

(36)

where
\mathbf{b}_{ij}
and
\mathbf{b}_{ji}
are defined in
eq.

12
.

Step 1:
0\leq\alpha_{2}\leq k-l
and
|\alpha|<\min\{s-l-2/p,k+1\}
.
Manipulating definitions shows that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{\alpha_{1}+r}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{l}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{r}}=\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{\otimes r}\cdot\frac{\partial^{\alpha_{1}}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{l+r}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}}\qquad 0\leq r\leq k-l,

(37)

and so there holds

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{|\alpha|}f^{l}_{j}}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\alpha_{2}}}=\mathbf{n}_{j}^{\otimes l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{|\alpha|}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{l}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\alpha_{2}}}=\mathbf{n}_{j}^{\otimes l}\cdot\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\otimes\alpha_{2}}\cdot\frac{\partial^{\alpha_{1}}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{l+\alpha_{2}}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}},

and using that
\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{l+\alpha_{2}}(F)|_{\Gamma_{i}}=0
by (i) gives

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{|\alpha|}f^{l}_{j}}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\alpha_{2}}}\circ\bm{F}_{ji}=\mathbf{n}_{j}^{\otimes l}\cdot\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\otimes\alpha_{2}}\cdot\left(\frac{\partial^{\alpha_{1}}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{l+\alpha_{2}}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}}\circ\bm{F}_{ji}-\frac{\partial^{\alpha_{1}}\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{l+\alpha_{2}}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}}\circ\bm{F}_{ij}\right)

(38)

on
T
. Equality
eq.

36
now follows from
eq.

18a
.

Step 2:
k-l+1\leq\alpha_{2}\leq k
and
|\alpha|<\min\{s-l-2/p,k+1\}
.
The same arguments as in Step 1 show that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{|\alpha|}f^{l}_{j}}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\alpha_{2}}}=\mathbf{n}_{j}^{\otimes l}\cdot\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\otimes k-l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{|\alpha|-k+l}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{k}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\alpha_{2}-k+l}}.

By construction, there exist constants
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
such that
\mathbf{n}_{j}=a_{1}\mathbf{b}_{ij}+a_{2}\mathbf{b}_{ji}
, and so

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{|\alpha|}f^{l}_{j}}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\alpha_{2}}}=\sum_{r=0}^{l}c_{r}\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{\otimes r}\cdot\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\otimes k-r}\cdot\frac{\partial^{|\alpha|-k+l}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{k}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{|\alpha|-k+l}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{l}c_{r}\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{\otimes r}\cdot\frac{\partial^{|\alpha|+l-r}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{r}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\alpha_{2}+l-r}}

for some suitable constants
\{c_{r}\}_{r=0}^{l}
. For
0\leq r\leq l-1
,
\mathfrak{D}^{r}_{j}(F)=0
by (ii), and so

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{|\alpha|}f^{l}_{j}}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\alpha_{2}}}=c_{l}\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{\otimes l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{|\alpha|}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{l}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\alpha_{2}}}

\displaystyle=c_{l}\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{\otimes l}\cdot\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\otimes k-l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{|\alpha|-k+l}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{k}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\alpha_{2}-k+l}}

\displaystyle=c_{l}\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{\otimes k-\alpha_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\otimes k-l}\cdot\left(\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{\otimes\alpha_{2}-k+l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{|\alpha|-k+l}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{k}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\alpha_{2}-k+l}}\right).

Applying (i) then gives the following identity on
T
:

\frac{\partial^{|\alpha|}f^{l}_{j}}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\alpha_{2}}}\circ\bm{F}_{ji}=c_{l}\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{\otimes k-\alpha_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\otimes k-l}\\
\cdot\left(\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{\otimes\alpha_{2}-k+l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{|\alpha|-k+l}\mathfrak{D}_{j}^{k}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\alpha_{2}-k+l}}\circ\bm{F}_{ji}-\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\otimes\alpha_{2}-k+l}\cdot\frac{\partial^{|\alpha|-k+l}\mathfrak{D}_{i}^{k}(F)}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ij}^{\alpha_{2}-k+l}}\circ\bm{F}_{ij}\right).

(39)

Equality
eq.

36
then follows from
eq.

18a
.

Step 3:
f_{j}^{l}\in W^{s-l-\frac{1}{p},p}(\Gamma_{j})\cap W^{\min\{s-l-\frac{1}{p},k+1\}p}_{\gamma_{ij}}(\Gamma_{j})
.
For
s-2/p\notin\mathbb{Z}
, the inclusion
f_{j}^{l}\in W^{s-l-\frac{1}{p},p}(\Gamma_{j})\cap W^{\min\{s-l-\frac{1}{p},k+1\},p}_{\gamma_{ij}}(\Gamma_{j})
follows from
eq.

36
, and
eqs.

35a
and
35b
are an immediate consequence of the definition of the
\|\cdot\|_{\Tr_{k}^{s,p},\Gamma_{i}\cup\Gamma_{j}}
norm. For
s-2/p\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
|\alpha|=\min\{s-l-2/p,k+1\}
, there holds

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma_{j}}\left|\frac{\partial^{|\alpha|}f^{l}_{j}}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\alpha_{2}}}(\bm{x})\right|^{p}\frac{\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}}{\dist(\bm{x},\gamma_{ij})}

\displaystyle\approx_{p}\int_{T}\left|\frac{\partial^{|\alpha|}f^{l}_{j}}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial\mathbf{b}_{ji}^{\alpha_{2}}}\right|^{p}\circ\bm{F}_{ji}(\bm{x})\frac{\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}}{x_{2}},

(40)

and so the inclusion
f_{j}^{l}\in W^{\min\{s-l-\frac{1}{p},k+1\},p}_{\gamma_{ij}}(\Gamma_{j})
follows from
eqs.

38
,
39
, and
18b
, while
eq.

35a
follows from the definition of the norm.

3.2.2
Construction of lifting

In the following lemma, we construct the lifting operator
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[2]}
in the same fashion as
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}

eq.

25
, replacing the use of
\mathcal{E}_{m}^{[1]}
with
\mathcal{M}_{m,k+1}^{[2]}
.

Lemma 13
.

Let
b\in C_{c}^{\infty}(T)
with
\int_{T}b(\bm{x})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}=1
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, and
\mathcal{S}=\{1,2\}
. For
F=(f^{0},f^{1},\ldots,f^{k})\in L^{p}(\Gamma_{1}\cup\Gamma_{2})^{k+1}
, we formally define the following operators:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{k,0}^{[2]}(F)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}(F)+\mathcal{M}_{0,k+1}^{[2]}(f_{2}^{0}-\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{2}}),\qquad

(41a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[2]}(F)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{L}_{k,m-1}^{[2]}(F)+\mathcal{M}_{m,k+1}^{[2]}(f_{2}^{m}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\mathcal{L}_{k,m-1}^{[2]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{2}}),\qquad

\displaystyle 1\leq m\leq k,

(41b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[2]}(F)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{L}_{k,k}^{[2]}(F).\qquad

(41c)

Then, for all
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
and
F\in\Tr_{k}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}})
,
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[2]}(F)
is well-defined and there holds

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[2]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{j}}=f_{j}^{m},\ \ 0\leq m\leq k,\ j\in\mathcal{S},\ \ \text{and}\ \ \|\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[2]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,s,p}\|F\|_{\Tr_{k}^{s,p},\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}}.

(42)

Moreover, if for some
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
F
satisfies
eq.

20
, then
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[2]}(F)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
.

Proof.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
, and
f\in\Tr_{k}^{s,p}(\mathcal{S})
be given.

Step 1:
m=0
.
Thanks to
lemma

9
, the traces
G=(g^{0},g^{1},\ldots,g^{k})
given by

\displaystyle g_{i}^{l}:=f_{i}^{l}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{i}},\qquad 0\leq l\leq k,\ 1\leq i\leq 2,

satisfy the hypotheses of
lemma

12
with
(i,j)=(1,2)
and
l=1
. Thanks to
lemma

12
and
corollary

11
,
\mathcal{M}_{0,k+1}^{[2]}(g_{2}^{0})
, and hence
\mathcal{L}_{k,0}^{[2]}(F)
, is well-defined with

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}_{k,0}^{[2]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,s,p}\|\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}+\begin{cases}\hskip-4.26773pt\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{12}}}}}\left\|f_{2}^{0}-\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-41.14877pt\kern 41.53766pt{}}}_{{\kern-48.18564pt{\gamma_{12}}\kern 41.53766pt{s-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{2}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{12}}}}}\left\|f_{2}^{0}-\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-41.14877pt\kern 41.53766pt{}}}_{{\kern-48.18564pt{\gamma_{12}}\kern 41.53766pt{s-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{2}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{12}}}}}\left\|f_{2}^{0}-\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-26.22038pt\kern 26.60927pt{}}}_{{\kern-32.53244pt{\gamma_{12}}\kern 26.60927pt{s-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{2}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{12}}}}}\left\|f_{2}^{0}-\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-20.45901pt\kern 20.8479pt{}}}_{{\kern-26.77107pt{\gamma_{12}}\kern 20.8479pt{s-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{2}}}}}&\hskip-5.69046pt\text{if }s\leq k+1+\frac{1}{p},\\
\|f_{2}^{0}-\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}(F)\|_{s-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{2}}&\hskip-5.69046pt\text{if }s>k+1+\frac{1}{p}.\end{cases}

Applying
eq.

26
and
eq.

33a
gives

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{l}\mathcal{L}_{k,0}^{[2]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{1}}=f_{1}^{l},\qquad 0\leq l\leq k,\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{L}_{k,0}^{[2]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{2}}=f_{2}^{0},

and applying
eq.

26
and
eq.

35
gives

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}_{k,0}^{[2]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,s,p}\|F\|_{\Tr_{k}^{s,p},\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}}.

Moreover, if
F
satisfies
eq.

20
and for some
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then
F\in\Tr^{s,p}_{k}(\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}})
by
lemma

5
and
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[1]}(F)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
by
lemma

9
. Thus, the trace
G
satisfies
eq.

20
for
\{i,j\}\subseteq\{1,2\}
and
G\in\Tr^{s,p}_{k}(\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}})
for all
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
. By
lemma

12
,
g_{2}^{0}\in W_{\gamma_{12}}^{k+1,p}(\Gamma_{2})
for all
p\in(1,\infty)
, and so
D_{\Gamma}^{l}g^{0}_{2}|_{\gamma_{12}}=0
for
0\leq l\leq k
. Thanks to
corollary

11
,
\mathcal{L}_{k,0}^{[2]}(F)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
.

Step 2: Induction on
m
.
Assume that for some
m
such that
0\leq m\leq k-1
,
\mathcal{L}^{[2]}_{k,m}(F)
is well-defined and satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{l}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[2]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{1}}=f_{1}^{l},\qquad 0\leq l\leq k,\qquad\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{l}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[2]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{2}}=f_{2}^{l},\qquad 0\leq l\leq m,

(43)

and

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[2]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,s,p}\|F\|_{\Tr_{k}^{s,p},\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}}.

(44)

Additionally assume that if
F
satisfies
eq.

20
for
\{i,j\}\subseteq\{1,2\}
and for some
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then
\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[2]}(F)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
.

Thanks to
eq.

43
, the traces
G=(g^{0},g^{1},\ldots,g^{k})
given by

\displaystyle g_{i}^{l}:=f_{i}^{l}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{l}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[2]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{i}},\qquad 0\leq l\leq k,\ 1\leq i\leq 2,

satisfy the hypotheses of
lemma

12
with
(i,j)=(1,2)
and
l=m+1
. Thanks to
lemma

12
and
corollary

11
,
\mathcal{M}_{m+1,k+1}^{[2]}(g_{2}^{m+1})
, and hence
\mathcal{L}_{k,m+1}^{[2]}(F)
is well-defined with

\|\mathcal{L}_{k,m+1}^{[2]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,s,p}\|\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[2]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}\\
+\begin{cases}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{12}}}}}\left\|f_{2}^{m+1}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m+1}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[2]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-69.61333pt\kern 70.00221pt{}}}_{{\kern-76.65019pt{\gamma_{12}}\kern 70.00221pt{s-m-1-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{2}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{12}}}}}\left\|f_{2}^{m+1}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m+1}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[2]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-69.61333pt\kern 70.00221pt{}}}_{{\kern-76.65019pt{\gamma_{12}}\kern 70.00221pt{s-m-1-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{2}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{12}}}}}\left\|f_{2}^{m+1}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m+1}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[2]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-44.94547pt\kern 45.33435pt{}}}_{{\kern-51.25752pt{\gamma_{12}}\kern 45.33435pt{s-m-1-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{2}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{12}}}}}\left\|f_{2}^{m+1}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m+1}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[2]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-38.44083pt\kern 38.82971pt{}}}_{{\kern-44.75288pt{\gamma_{12}}\kern 38.82971pt{s-m-1-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{2}}}}}&\text{if }s-m-1\leq k+1+\frac{1}{p},\\
\|f_{2}^{m+1}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m+1}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[2]}(F)\|_{s-m-1-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{2}}&\text{if }s-m-1>k+1+\frac{1}{p}.\end{cases}

Applying
eq.

43
and
eq.

33a
gives
eq.

43
for
m+1
, while applying
eq.

44
and
eq.

35
gives
eq.

44
for
m+1
.

Moreover, if
F
satisfies
eq.

20
for some
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then
\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[2]}(F)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
by assumption and so the trace
G
satisfies
eq.

20
and
G\in\Tr^{s,p}_{k}(\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}})
for all
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
. By
lemma

12
,
g_{2}^{m+1}\in W_{\gamma_{12}}^{k+1,p}(\Gamma_{2})
for all
p\in(1,\infty)
, and so
D_{\Gamma}^{l}g^{m+1}_{2}|_{\gamma_{12}}=0
for
0\leq l\leq k
. Thanks to
corollary

11
,
\mathcal{L}_{k,m+1}^{[2]}(F)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
.

3.3
Lifting from three faces

We continue in the spirit of the previous two sections and define another lifting operator from
\Gamma_{1}
with vanishing traces on
\Gamma_{2}
and
\Gamma_{3}
. Given nonnegative integers
k,r\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, a smooth compactly supported function
b\in C_{c}^{\infty}(T)
, and a function
f:T\to\mathbb{R}
, we define the operator
\mathcal{S}_{k,r}^{[1]}
formally by the rule

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{S}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f)(\bm{x},z)&:=(x_{1}x_{2})^{r}\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}((\omega_{1}\omega_{2})^{-r}f)(\bm{x},z)\\
&=(x_{1}x_{2})^{r}\frac{(-z)^{k}}{k!}\int_{T}\frac{b(\bm{y})f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})}{((x_{1}+zy_{1})(x_{2}+zy_{2}))^{r}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}\qquad\forall(\bm{x},z)\in K.\end{aligned}

(45)

Note that when
r=0
, we have
\mathcal{S}_{k,0}^{[1]}=\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}
. For functions
f:\Gamma_{1}\to\mathbb{R}
, we again abuse the notation and set
\mathcal{S}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f):=\mathcal{S}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f\circ\mathfrak{I}_{1})
.

We require one additional family of spaces with vanishing traces. Let
s=m+\sigma
with
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\sigma\in[0,1)
and
1<p<\infty
. Given
r\in\mathbb{N}
, a face
\Gamma_{j}
,
1\leq j\leq 4
, and
\mathfrak{E}
a subset of the edges of
\Gamma_{j}
, we define the following subspaces of
W^{s,p}_{\mathfrak{E}}(\Gamma_{j})
:

\displaystyle W^{s,p}_{\mathfrak{E},r}(\Gamma_{j}):=\left\{f\in W^{s,p}(\Gamma_{j})\cap W^{\min\{s,r\},p}_{\mathfrak{E}}(\Gamma_{j}):\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-20.03227pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,T}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-20.03227pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,T}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-7.4375pt\kern 7.82639pt{}}}_{{\kern-12.96793pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 7.82639pt{s,p,T}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-5.2014pt\kern 5.59029pt{}}}_{{\kern-10.73183pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 5.59029pt{s,p,T}}}}<\infty\right\},

(46)

where the norm on
W^{s,p}_{\mathfrak{E},r}(\Gamma_{j})
is given by

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{p}}}_{{\kern-20.03227pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{p}}}_{{\kern-20.03227pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-7.4375pt\kern 7.82639pt{p}}}_{{\kern-12.96793pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 7.82639pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-5.2014pt\kern 5.59029pt{p}}}_{{\kern-10.73183pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 5.59029pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}:=&\begin{cases}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{p}}}_{{\kern-16.32437pt{\mathfrak{E}}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,T}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{p}}}_{{\kern-16.32437pt{\mathfrak{E}}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,T}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-7.4375pt\kern 7.82639pt{p}}}_{{\kern-10.31944pt{\mathfrak{E}}\kern 7.82639pt{s,p,T}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-5.2014pt\kern 5.59029pt{p}}}_{{\kern-8.08334pt{\mathfrak{E}}\kern 5.59029pt{s,p,T}}}}&\text{if }s\leq r,\\
\|f\|_{s,p,T}^{p}&\text{if }s>r,\end{cases}\\
&+\begin{cases}\left\|\dist(\cdot,\bigcup_{\gamma\in\mathfrak{E}}\gamma)^{-\sigma}\frac{\partial^{m-r+1}D_{\Gamma}^{r-1}f}{\partial\mathbf{t}_{\gamma}^{m-r+1}}\right\|_{p,\Gamma_{j}}^{p}&\text{if }s>r,\ \sigma p=1,\mathfrak{E}\neq\emptyset,\\
0&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}\end{aligned}

where
\mathbf{t}_{\gamma}
is a unit-tangent vector on the edge
\gamma\in\mathfrak{E}
. For
r=0
, we set
W_{\mathfrak{E},0}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{j}):=W^{s,p}(\Gamma_{j})
. When
\mathfrak{E}
consists of only one element
\gamma
, we set
W_{\gamma,r}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{j}):=W_{\mathfrak{E},r}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{j})
and
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma,r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-19.23442pt{\gamma,r}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma,r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-19.23442pt{\gamma,r}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma,r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-7.4375pt\kern 7.82639pt{}}}_{{\kern-12.39804pt{\gamma,r}\kern 7.82639pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma,r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-5.2014pt\kern 5.59029pt{}}}_{{\kern-10.16194pt{\gamma,r}\kern 5.59029pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}:=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-20.03227pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-20.03227pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-7.4375pt\kern 7.82639pt{}}}_{{\kern-12.96793pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 7.82639pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-5.2014pt\kern 5.59029pt{}}}_{{\kern-10.73183pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 5.59029pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}
. One can again verify that
W_{\mathfrak{E},r}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{j})
are Banach spaces and that the following analogue of
eq.

30
holds:

\displaystyle W_{\mathfrak{E},r}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{j})=\bigcap_{\gamma\in\mathfrak{E}}W_{\gamma,r}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{j})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-20.03227pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-20.03227pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-7.4375pt\kern 7.82639pt{}}}_{{\kern-12.96793pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 7.82639pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-5.2014pt\kern 5.59029pt{}}}_{{\kern-10.73183pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 5.59029pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}\approx_{s,p}\sum_{\gamma\in\mathfrak{E}}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma,r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-19.23442pt{\gamma,r}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma,r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-19.23442pt{\gamma,r}\kern 12.87854pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma,r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-7.4375pt\kern 7.82639pt{}}}_{{\kern-12.39804pt{\gamma,r}\kern 7.82639pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma,r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-5.2014pt\kern 5.59029pt{}}}_{{\kern-10.16194pt{\gamma,r}\kern 5.59029pt{s,p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}.

(47)

The following result shows that the continuity of
\mathcal{S}_{k,r}^{[1]}
can be characterized with these spaces.

Lemma 14
.

Let
b\in C_{c}^{\infty}(T)
,
k,r\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
, and
\mathfrak{E}=\{\gamma_{12},\gamma_{13}\}
. Then, for all
f\in W^{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p}_{\mathfrak{E},r}(\Gamma_{1})
, there holds

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\mathcal{S}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f)|_{\Gamma_{1}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{km}\left(\int_{T}b(\bm{x})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\right)f,\qquad

\displaystyle 0\leq m\leq k,

(48a)

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{j}\mathcal{S}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f)|_{\Gamma_{i}}

\displaystyle=0,\qquad

\displaystyle 0\leq j<\min\left\{r,s-\frac{1}{p}\right\},\ 2\leq i\leq 3,

(48b)

and

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,r,s,p}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-20.03227pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 12.87854pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{1}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-20.03227pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 12.87854pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{1}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-7.4375pt\kern 7.82639pt{}}}_{{\kern-12.96793pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 7.82639pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{1}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-5.2014pt\kern 5.59029pt{}}}_{{\kern-10.73183pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 5.59029pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{1}}}}}.

(49)

Moreover, if
f\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(\Gamma_{1})
,
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, satisfies
D_{\Gamma}^{l}f|_{\gamma_{12}}=D_{\Gamma}^{l}f|_{\gamma_{13}}=0
for
0\leq l\leq r-1
, then
\mathcal{S}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f)\in\mathcal{P}_{N+k}(K)
.

The proof of
lemma

14
appears in
section

6.4
. We define the analogous operator associated to
\Gamma_{3}
as follows.

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{k,r}^{[3]}(f)(\bm{x},z):=\mathcal{S}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f\circ\mathfrak{I}_{3})\circ\mathfrak{R}_{13}(\bm{x},z)\qquad\forall(\bm{x},z)\in K,

where
\mathfrak{I}_{3}(\bm{x}):=(0,x_{2},x_{1})
and
\mathfrak{R}_{13}(\bm{x},z):=(z,x_{2},x_{1})
for all
(\bm{x},z)\in K
. Thanks to the chain rule and the smoothness of the mappings
\mathfrak{I}_{3}
and
\mathfrak{R}_{13}
, the continuity and interpolation properties of
\mathcal{S}_{k,r}^{[3]}
follow immediately from
lemma

14
.

Corollary 15
.

Let
b\in C_{c}^{\infty}(T)
,
k,r\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
, and
\mathfrak{E}=\{\gamma_{13},\gamma_{23}\}
. Then, for all
f\in W^{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p}_{\mathfrak{E},r}(\Gamma_{3})
, there holds

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\mathcal{S}_{k,r}^{[3]}(f)|_{\Gamma_{3}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{km}\left(\int_{T}b(\bm{x})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\right)f,\qquad

\displaystyle 0\leq m\leq k,

(50a)

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{j}\mathcal{S}_{k,r}^{[3]}(f)|_{\Gamma_{i}}

\displaystyle=0,\qquad

\displaystyle 0\leq j<\min\left\{r,s-\frac{1}{p}\right\},\ 1\leq i\leq 2

(50b)

and

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}_{k,r}^{[3]}(f)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,r,s,p}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-20.03227pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 12.87854pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{3}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-20.03227pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 12.87854pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{3}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-7.4375pt\kern 7.82639pt{}}}_{{\kern-12.96793pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 7.82639pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{3}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-5.2014pt\kern 5.59029pt{}}}_{{\kern-10.73183pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 5.59029pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{3}}}}}.

(51)

Moreover, if
f\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(\Gamma_{3})
,
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, satisfies
D_{\Gamma}^{l}f|_{\gamma_{13}}=D_{\Gamma}^{l}f|_{\gamma_{23}}=0
for
0\leq l\leq r-1
, then
\mathcal{S}_{k,r}^{[3]}(f)\in\mathcal{P}_{N+k}(K)
.

We also have the following analogue of
lemma

12
.

Lemma 16
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
,
1\leq l\leq k
, and
1\leq i,j\leq 4
with
i\neq j
be given. Suppose that
F=(f^{0},f^{1},\ldots,f^{k})\in\Tr_{k}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{i}\cup\Gamma_{j})
satisfies

(i)

F=(0,0,\ldots,0)
on
\Gamma_{i}
;

(ii)

f_{j}^{m}=0
on
\Gamma_{j}
for
0\leq m\leq l-1
.

Then, there holds
f^{l}_{j}\in W_{\gamma_{ij},k+1}^{s-l-\frac{1}{p},p}(\Gamma_{j})
and

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{ij},k+1}}}}\left\|f_{j}^{l}\right\|^{{\kern-15.288pt\kern 15.67688pt{}}}_{{\kern-31.99974pt{\gamma_{ij},k+1}\kern 15.67688pt{s-l-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{ij},k+1}}}}\left\|f_{j}^{l}\right\|^{{\kern-15.288pt\kern 15.67688pt{}}}_{{\kern-31.99974pt{\gamma_{ij},k+1}\kern 15.67688pt{s-l-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{ij},k+1}}}}\left\|f_{j}^{l}\right\|^{{\kern-9.57916pt\kern 9.96805pt{}}}_{{\kern-22.6154pt{\gamma_{ij},k+1}\kern 9.96805pt{s-l-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\gamma_{ij},k+1}}}}\left\|f_{j}^{l}\right\|^{{\kern-7.34306pt\kern 7.73195pt{}}}_{{\kern-20.3793pt{\gamma_{ij},k+1}\kern 7.73195pt{s-l-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{j}}}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{k,s,p}\|F\|_{\Tr_{k}^{s,p},\Gamma_{i}\cup\Gamma_{j}}.

(52)

Proof.

The result follows from applying inequality
eq.

40
and identity
eq.

39
.

We now construct the lifting operator
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[3]}
in the same fashion as
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[2]}

eq.

41
, replacing the use of
\mathcal{M}_{m,k}^{[2]}
with
\mathcal{S}_{m,k}^{[3]}
.

Lemma 17
.

Let
b\in C_{c}^{\infty}(T)
with
\int_{T}b(\bm{x})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}=1
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, and
\mathcal{S}=\{1,2,3\}
. For
F=(f^{0},f^{1},\ldots,f^{k})\in L^{p}(\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}})^{k+1}
, we formally define the following operators:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{k,0}^{[3]}(F)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[2]}(F)+\mathcal{S}_{0,k+1}^{[3]}(f_{3}^{0}-\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[2]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{3}}),\qquad

(53a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[3]}(F)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{L}_{k,m-1}^{[3]}(F)+\mathcal{S}_{m,k+1}^{[3]}(f_{3}^{m}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\mathcal{L}_{k,m-1}^{[3]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{3}}),\qquad

\displaystyle 1\leq m\leq k,

(53b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[3]}(F)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{L}_{k,k}^{[3]}(F).

(53c)

Then, for all
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
and
F\in\Tr_{k}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}})
, there holds

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[3]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{j}}=f_{j}^{m},\quad 0\leq m\leq k,\ j\in\mathcal{S},\quad\text{and}\quad\|\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[3]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,s,p}\|F\|_{\Tr_{k}^{s,p},\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}}.

(54)

Moreover, if
F
satisfies
eq.

20
and for some
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[3]}(F)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
.

Proof.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
, and
f\in\Tr_{k}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}})
be given. Let
\mathfrak{E}=\{\gamma_{13},\gamma_{23}\}
.

Step 1:
m=0
.
Thanks to
lemma

13
, the traces
G=(g^{0},g^{1},\ldots,g^{k})
given by

\displaystyle g_{i}^{l}:=f_{i}^{l}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[2]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{i}},\qquad 0\leq l\leq k,\ 1\leq i\leq 3,

satisfy the hypotheses of
lemma

16
with
(i,j)\in\{(1,3),(2,3)\}
and
l=1
. Thanks to
eq.

47
and
lemma

16
,
g^{0}_{3}\in W_{\mathfrak{E},k+1}^{s-\frac{1}{p},p}(\Gamma_{3})
. Consequently,
\mathcal{S}_{0,k+1}^{[3]}(g_{3}^{0})
is well-defined by
corollary

15
, and hence
\mathcal{L}_{k,0}^{[3]}(F)
is well-defined with

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}_{k,0}^{[3]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,s,p}\|\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[2]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}+\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},k+1}}}}\left\|f_{3}^{0}-\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[2]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-41.14877pt\kern 41.53766pt{}}}_{{\kern-55.31091pt{\mathfrak{E},k+1}\kern 41.53766pt{s-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{3}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},k+1}}}}\left\|f_{3}^{0}-\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[2]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-41.14877pt\kern 41.53766pt{}}}_{{\kern-55.31091pt{\mathfrak{E},k+1}\kern 41.53766pt{s-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{3}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},k+1}}}}\left\|f_{3}^{0}-\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[2]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-26.22038pt\kern 26.60927pt{}}}_{{\kern-36.47905pt{\mathfrak{E},k+1}\kern 26.60927pt{s-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{3}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},k+1}}}}\left\|f_{3}^{0}-\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[2]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-20.45901pt\kern 20.8479pt{}}}_{{\kern-30.71768pt{\mathfrak{E},k+1}\kern 20.8479pt{s-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{3}}}}}.

Applying
eq.

42
and
eq.

50a
gives

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{l}\mathcal{L}_{k,0}^{[3]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{i}}=f_{i}^{l},\qquad 0\leq l\leq k,\ 1\leq i\leq 2,\qquad\mathcal{L}_{k,0}^{[3]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{3}}=f_{3}^{0},

and applying
eq.

30
,
eq.

42
, and
eq.

35
gives

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}_{k,0}^{[3]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,s,p}\|F\|_{\Tr_{k}^{s,p},\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}}.

Moreover, if
F
satisfies
eq.

20
for some
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[2]}(F)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
by
lemma

13
, and so the trace
G
satisfies
eq.

20
and
G\in\Tr^{s,p}_{k}(\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}})
for all
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
thanks to
lemma

5
. By
eq.

47
and
lemma

16
,
g_{3}^{0}\in W_{\mathfrak{E}}^{k+1,p}(\Gamma_{3})
for all
p\in(1,\infty)
, and so
D_{\Gamma}^{l}g^{0}_{3}|_{\gamma_{13}}=D_{\Gamma}^{l}g^{0}_{3}|_{\gamma_{23}}=0
for
0\leq l\leq k
. Thanks to
corollary

15
,
\mathcal{L}_{k,0}^{[3]}(F)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
.

Step 2: Induction on
m
.
Assume that for some
m
such that
0\leq m\leq k-1
,
\mathcal{L}^{[3]}_{k,m}(F)
is well-defined and satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{l}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[3]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{i}}

\displaystyle=f_{i}^{l},\qquad

\displaystyle 0\leq l\leq k,\ 1\leq i\leq 2,

(55a)

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{l}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[3]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{3}}

\displaystyle=f_{3}^{l},\qquad

\displaystyle 0\leq l\leq m,

(55b)

and

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[3]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,s,p}\|F\|_{\Tr_{k}^{s,p},\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}}.

(56)

Additionally assume that if
F
satisfies
eq.

20
for some
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then
\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[3]}(F)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
.

Thanks to
eq.

55
, the traces
G=(g^{0},g^{1},\ldots,g^{k})
given by

\displaystyle g_{i}^{l}:=f_{i}^{l}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{l}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[3]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{i}},\qquad 0\leq l\leq k,\ 1\leq i\leq 3,

satisfy the hypotheses of
lemma

16
with
(i,j)\in\{(1,3),(2,3)\}
and
l=m+1
. Thanks to
eq.

47
and
lemma

16
, there holds
g^{m+1}_{3}\in W_{\mathfrak{E},k+1}^{s-m-1-\frac{1}{p},p}(\Gamma_{3})
. Consequently,
\mathcal{S}_{m+1,k+1}^{[3]}(g_{3}^{m+1})
is well-defined by
corollary

15
, and hence
\mathcal{L}_{k,m+1}^{[3]}(F)
is well-defined with

\|\mathcal{L}_{k,m+1}^{[3]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,s,p}\|\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[3]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}\\
