+\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},k+1}}}}\left\|f_{3}^{m+1}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m+1}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[3]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-69.61333pt\kern 70.00221pt{}}}_{{\kern-83.77547pt{\mathfrak{E},k+1}\kern 70.00221pt{s-m-1-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{3}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},k+1}}}}\left\|f_{3}^{m+1}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m+1}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[3]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-69.61333pt\kern 70.00221pt{}}}_{{\kern-83.77547pt{\mathfrak{E},k+1}\kern 70.00221pt{s-m-1-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{3}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},k+1}}}}\left\|f_{3}^{m+1}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m+1}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[3]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-44.94547pt\kern 45.33435pt{}}}_{{\kern-55.20413pt{\mathfrak{E},k+1}\kern 45.33435pt{s-m-1-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{3}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},k+1}}}}\left\|f_{3}^{m+1}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m+1}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[3]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-38.44083pt\kern 38.82971pt{}}}_{{\kern-48.6995pt{\mathfrak{E},k+1}\kern 38.82971pt{s-m-1-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{3}}}}}.

Applying
eq.

55
and
eq.

50a
gives
eq.

55
for
m+1
, while applying
eq.

47
,
eq.

56
, and
eq.

52
gives
eq.

56
for
m+1
.

Moreover, if
F
satisfies
eq.

20
for some
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then
\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[3]}(F)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
by assumption and so the trace
G
satisfies
eq.

20
and
G\in\Tr^{s,p}_{k}(\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}})
for all
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
thanks to
lemma

5
. By
eq.

30
and
lemma

12
,
g_{3}^{m+1}\in W_{\mathfrak{E}}^{k+1,p}(\Gamma_{3})
for all
p\in(1,\infty)
, and so
D_{\Gamma}^{l}g^{m+1}_{3}|_{\gamma_{13}}=D_{\Gamma}^{l}g_{3}^{m+1}|_{\gamma_{23}}=0
for
0\leq l\leq k
. Thanks to
corollary

15
,
\mathcal{L}_{k,m+1}^{[3]}(F)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
.

3.4
Lifting from four faces

To complete the construction of the lifting operator from the entire boundary, we
define one final single face lifting operator from
\Gamma_{1}
that vanishes on the remaining faces. Given nonnegative integers
k,r\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, a smooth compactly supported function
b\in C_{c}^{\infty}(T)
, and a function
f:T\to\mathbb{R}
, we define the operator
\mathcal{R}_{k,r}^{[1]}
formally by the rule

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{R}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f)(\bm{x},z)\\
&\quad:=(x_{1}x_{2}(1-x_{1}-x_{2}-z))^{r}\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}((\omega_{1}\omega_{2}\omega_{3})^{-r}f)(\bm{x},z)\\
&\quad=(x_{1}x_{2}(1-x_{1}-x_{2}-z))^{r}\frac{(-z)^{k}}{k!}\int_{T}\left.\frac{b(\bm{y})f(\bm{w})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}}{(\omega_{1}\omega_{2}\omega_{3})^{r}(\bm{w})}\right|_{\bm{w}=\bm{x}+z\bm{y}}\ \forall(\bm{x},z)\in K.\end{aligned}

(57)

Note that when
r=0
, we have
\mathcal{R}_{k,r}^{[1]}=\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}
. For functions
f:\Gamma_{1}\to\mathbb{R}
, we again abuse notation and set
\mathcal{R}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f):=\mathcal{R}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f\circ\mathfrak{I}_{1})
. The weighted spaces
W_{\mathfrak{E},r}^{s,p}(\Gamma_{1})
again play a role in the continuity of
\mathcal{R}_{k,r}^{[1]}
as the following result shows.

Lemma 18
.

Let
b\in C_{c}^{\infty}(T)
,
k,r\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
, and
\mathfrak{E}=\{\gamma_{12},\gamma_{13},\gamma_{14}\}
. Then, for all
f\in W_{\mathfrak{E},r}^{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p}(\Gamma_{1})
, there holds

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\mathcal{R}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f)|_{\Gamma_{1}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{km}\left(\int_{T}b(\bm{x})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\right)f,\qquad

\displaystyle 0\leq m\leq k,

(58a)

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{j}\mathcal{R}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f)|_{\Gamma_{i}}

\displaystyle=0,\qquad

\displaystyle 0\leq j<\min\left\{r,s-\frac{1}{p}\right\},\ 2\leq i\leq 4,

(58b)

and

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,r,s,p}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-20.03227pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 12.87854pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{1}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-20.03227pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 12.87854pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{1}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-7.4375pt\kern 7.82639pt{}}}_{{\kern-12.96793pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 7.82639pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{1}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-5.2014pt\kern 5.59029pt{}}}_{{\kern-10.73183pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 5.59029pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{1}}}}}.

(59)

Moreover, if
f\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(\Gamma_{1})
,
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, satisfies
D_{\Gamma}^{l}f|_{\partial\Gamma_{1}}=0
for
0\leq l\leq r-1
, then
\mathcal{R}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f)\in\mathcal{P}_{N+k}(K)
.

The proof of
lemma

18
appears in
section

6.5
. The analogous operator associated to
\Gamma_{4}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{k,r}^{[4]}(f)(\bm{x},z):=3^{-\frac{k}{2}}\mathcal{R}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f\circ\mathfrak{I}_{4})\circ\mathfrak{R}_{14}(\bm{x},z)\qquad\forall(\bm{x},z)\in K,

where
\mathfrak{I}_{4}(\bm{x}):=(x_{1},x_{2},1-x_{1}-x_{2})
and
\mathfrak{R}_{14}(\bm{x},z):=(x_{1},x_{2},1-x_{1}-x_{2}-z)
for all
(\bm{x},z)\in K
. Thanks to the chain rule and the smoothness of the mappings
\mathfrak{I}_{4}
and
\mathfrak{R}_{14}
, the continuity and interpolation properties of
\mathcal{R}_{k,r}^{[4]}
follow immediately from
lemma

18
.

Corollary 19
.

Let
b\in C_{c}^{\infty}(T)
,
k,r\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
, and
\mathfrak{E}=\{\gamma_{14},\gamma_{24},\gamma_{34}\}
. Then, for all
f\in W_{\mathfrak{E},r}^{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p}(\Gamma_{4})
, there holds

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\mathcal{R}_{k,r}^{[4]}(f)|_{\Gamma_{4}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{km}\left(\int_{T}b(\bm{x})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\right)f,\qquad

\displaystyle 0\leq m\leq k,

(60a)

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{j}\mathcal{R}_{k,r}^{[4]}(f)|_{\Gamma_{i}}

\displaystyle=0,\qquad

\displaystyle 0\leq j<\min\left\{r,s-\frac{1}{p}\right\},\ 1\leq i\leq 3

(60b)

and

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}_{k,r}^{[4]}(f)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,r,s,p}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-20.03227pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 12.87854pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{4}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-12.48965pt\kern 12.87854pt{}}}_{{\kern-20.03227pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 12.87854pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{4}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-7.4375pt\kern 7.82639pt{}}}_{{\kern-12.96793pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 7.82639pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{4}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},r}}}}\left\|f\right\|^{{\kern-5.2014pt\kern 5.59029pt{}}}_{{\kern-10.73183pt{\mathfrak{E},r}\kern 5.59029pt{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{4}}}}}.

(61)

Moreover, if
f\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(\Gamma_{4})
,
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, satisfies
D_{\Gamma}^{l}f|_{\partial\Gamma_{4}}=0
for
0\leq l\leq r-1
, then
\mathcal{R}_{k,r}^{[4]}(f)\in\mathcal{P}_{N+k}(K)
.

Finally, we construct the lifting operator
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[4]}
in the same fashion as
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[3]}

eq.

53
, replacing the use of
\mathcal{S}_{m,k+1}^{[3]}
with
\mathcal{R}_{m,k+1}^{[4]}
.

Lemma 20
.

Let
b\in C_{c}^{\infty}(T)
with
\int_{T}b(\bm{x})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}=1
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. For
F=(f^{0},f^{1},\ldots,f^{k})\in L^{p}(\partial K)^{k+1}
, we formally define the following operators:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{k,0}^{[4]}(F)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[3]}(F)+\mathcal{R}_{0,k+1}^{[4]}(f_{4}^{0}-\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[3]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{4}}),\qquad

(62a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[4]}(F)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{L}_{k,m-1}^{[4]}(F)+\mathcal{R}_{m,k+1}^{[4]}(f_{4}^{m}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\mathcal{L}_{k,m-1}^{[4]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{4}}),\qquad

\displaystyle 1\leq m\leq k,

(62b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[4]}(F)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{L}_{k,k}^{[4]}(F).\qquad

(62c)

Then, for all
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
and
F\in\Tr_{k}^{s,p}(\partial K)
, there holds

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m}\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[4]}(F)|_{\partial K}=f^{m},\quad 0\leq m\leq k,\quad\text{and}\quad\|\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[4]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,s,p}\|F\|_{\Tr_{k}^{s,p},\partial K}.

(63)

Moreover, if
F
satisfies
eq.

20
for some
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[4]}(F)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
.

Proof.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
, and
f\in\Tr_{k}^{s,p}(\partial K)
be given and set
\mathfrak{E}:=\{\gamma_{14},\gamma_{24},\gamma_{34}\}
.

Step 1:
m=0
.
Thanks to
lemma

17
, the traces
G=(g^{0},g^{1},\ldots,g^{k})
given by

\displaystyle g_{i}^{l}:=f_{i}^{l}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[3]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{i}},\qquad 0\leq l\leq k,\ 1\leq i\leq 4,

satisfies the hypotheses of
lemma

16
with
(i,j)\in\{(1,4),(2,4),(3,4)\}
and

. Thanks to

47
and
lemma

,
g^{0}_{4}\in W_{\mathfrak{E},k+1}^{s-\frac{1}{p},p}(\Gamma_{4})
. Consequently,

, and hence

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}_{k,0}^{[4]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,s,p}\|\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[3]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}+\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},k+1}}}}\left\|f_{4}^{0}-\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[3]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-41.14877pt\kern 41.53766pt{}}}_{{\kern-55.31091pt{\mathfrak{E},k+1}\kern 41.53766pt{s-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{4}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},k+1}}}}\left\|f_{4}^{0}-\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[3]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-41.14877pt\kern 41.53766pt{}}}_{{\kern-55.31091pt{\mathfrak{E},k+1}\kern 41.53766pt{s-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{4}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},k+1}}}}\left\|f_{4}^{0}-\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[3]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-26.22038pt\kern 26.60927pt{}}}_{{\kern-36.47905pt{\mathfrak{E},k+1}\kern 26.60927pt{s-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{4}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},k+1}}}}\left\|f_{4}^{0}-\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[3]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-20.45901pt\kern 20.8479pt{}}}_{{\kern-30.71768pt{\mathfrak{E},k+1}\kern 20.8479pt{s-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{4}}}}}.

Applying
eq.

54
and
eq.

60a
gives

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{l}\mathcal{L}_{k,0}^{[4]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{i}}=f_{i}^{l},\qquad 0\leq l\leq k,\ 1\leq i\leq 3,\qquad\mathcal{L}_{k,0}^{[4]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{3}}=f_{4}^{0},

and applying
eq.

47
,
eq.

54
, and
eq.

52
gives

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}_{k,0}^{[4]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,s,p}\|F\|_{\Tr_{k}^{s,p},\partial K}.

Moreover, if
F
satisfies
eq.

20
for some
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[3]}(F)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
by
lemma

13
, and so the trace
G
satisfies
eq.

20
and
G\in\Tr^{s,p}_{k}(\partial K)
for all
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
thanks to
lemma

5
. By
eq.

47
and
lemma

16
,
g_{4}^{0}\in W_{\mathfrak{E}}^{k+1,p}(\Gamma_{4})
for all
p\in(1,\infty)
, and so

for
0\leq l\leq k
. Thanks to
corollary

19
,
\mathcal{L}_{k,0}^{[4]}(F)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
.

Step 2: Induction on
m
.
Assume that for some
0\leq m\leq k-1
,
\mathcal{L}^{[4]}_{k,m}(F)
is well-defined and satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{l}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[4]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{i}}

\displaystyle=f_{i}^{l},\qquad

\displaystyle 0\leq l\leq k,\ 1\leq i\leq 3,

(64a)

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{l}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[4]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{4}}

\displaystyle=f_{4}^{l},\qquad

\displaystyle 0\leq l\leq m,

(64b)

and

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[4]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,s,p}\|F\|_{\Tr_{k}^{s,p},\partial K}.

(65)

Additionally assume that if
F
satisfies
eq.

20
for some
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then
\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[4]}(F)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
.

Thanks to
eq.

64
, the traces
G=(g^{0},g^{1},\ldots,g^{k})
given by

\displaystyle g_{i}^{l}:=f_{i}^{l}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{l}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[4]}(F)|_{\Gamma_{i}},\qquad 0\leq l\leq k,\ 1\leq i\leq 4,

satisfies the hypotheses of
lemma

16
with
(i,j)\in\{(1,4),(2,4),(3,4)\}
and
l=m+1
. Thanks to
eq.

47
and
lemma

16
,
g^{m+1}_{4}\in W_{\mathfrak{E},k+1}^{s-m-1-\frac{1}{p},p}(\Gamma_{4})
. Consequently,
\mathcal{R}_{m+1,k+1}^{[4]}(g_{4}^{m+1})
is well-defined by

, and hence
\mathcal{L}_{k,m+1}^{[4]}(F)
is well-defined with

\|\mathcal{L}_{k,m+1}^{[4]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,s,p}\|\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[4]}(F)\|_{s,p,K}\\
+\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},k+1}}}}\left\|f_{4}^{m+1}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m+1}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[4]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-69.61333pt\kern 70.00221pt{}}}_{{\kern-83.77547pt{\mathfrak{E},k+1}\kern 70.00221pt{s-m-1-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{4}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},k+1}}}}\left\|f_{4}^{m+1}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m+1}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[4]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-69.61333pt\kern 70.00221pt{}}}_{{\kern-83.77547pt{\mathfrak{E},k+1}\kern 70.00221pt{s-m-1-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{4}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},k+1}}}}\left\|f_{4}^{m+1}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m+1}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[4]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-44.94547pt\kern 45.33435pt{}}}_{{\kern-55.20413pt{\mathfrak{E},k+1}\kern 45.33435pt{s-m-1-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{4}}}}}{\hphantom{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E},k+1}}}}\left\|f_{4}^{m+1}-\partial_{\mathbf{n}}^{m+1}\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[4]}(F)\right\|^{{\kern-38.44083pt\kern 38.82971pt{}}}_{{\kern-48.6995pt{\mathfrak{E},k+1}\kern 38.82971pt{s-m-1-\frac{1}{p},p,\Gamma_{4}}}}}.

Applying
eq.

64
and
eq.

60a
gives
eq.

64
for
m+1
, while applying
eq.

47
,
eq.

65
, and
eq.

52
gives
eq.

65
for
m+1
.

Moreover, if
F
satisfies
eq.

20
for some
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then
\mathcal{L}_{k,m}^{[4]}(F)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
by assumption and so the trace
G
satisfies
eq.

20
and
G\in\Tr^{s,p}_{k}(\partial K)
for all
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
thanks to
lemma

5
. By
eq.

47
and
lemma

16
,
g_{4}^{m+1}\in W_{\mathfrak{E}}^{k+1,p}(\Gamma_{4})
for all
p\in(1,\infty)
, and so
D_{\Gamma}^{l}g^{m+1}_{4}|_{\partial\Gamma_{4}}=0
for
0\leq l\leq k
. Thanks to
corollary

19
,
\mathcal{L}_{k,m+1}^{[4]}(F)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(K)
.

3.5
Proof of
theorem

6

Let
b\in C_{c}^{\infty}(T)
be any smooth function satisfying
\int_{T}b(\bm{x})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}=1
. Then,
\mathcal{L}_{k}:=\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[4]}
, where
\mathcal{L}_{k}^{[4]}
is defined in
eq.

62
satisfies the desired properties thanks to
lemma

20
.
\proofbox

4
Whole space operators

In this section, we examine the continuity properties of the following operators, which are the whole space extensions of the lifting operators
\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}

eq.

21
: Given
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
, and
b\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and a function
f:\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
, we define the lifting operator
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}
by the rule

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}(f)(\bm{x},z):=\chi(z)z^{k}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}b(\bm{y})f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}\qquad\forall(\bm{x},z)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}.

(66)

We use the notation
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}[\chi,b]
when we want to make the dependence on
\chi
and
b
explicit. The advantage of working with the operator
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}
is that we shall capitalize on the abundance of equivalent
W^{s,p}(U)
-norms when
U
is all of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
or the half-space
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}=\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times(0,\infty)
,
d>1
. In particular, we recall the following norm-equivalence on
W^{s,p}(U)
,
0<s<1
,
1<p<\infty
, with
U=\mathbb{R}^{d}
or
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}
(see e.g.
[
43
, Theorems 6.38 & 6.61]
):

\displaystyle|f|_{s,p,U}^{p}\approx_{s,p,d}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{U}\frac{|f(\bm{x}+t\mathbf{e}_{i})-f(\bm{x})|^{p}}{t^{1+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\qquad\forall f\in W^{s,p}(U).

(67)

The main result of this section is the following analogue of
lemma

8
.

Theorem 21
.

Let
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
with
\supp\chi\in(-2,2)
,
b\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
with
\supp b\subset T
, and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
be given. Then, for
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}\cup(k+\frac{1}{2},2)
, there holds

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}(f)\|_{s,p,\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\lesssim_{\chi,b,k,s,p}\|f\|_{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\mathbb{R}^{2}}\qquad\forall f\in W^{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p}(\mathbb{R}^{2}).

(68)

The proof of
theorem

21
appears in
section

4.3
.

4.1
Continuity of
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}

We begin by recording the particular case of
theorem

21
with
k=0
, which follows from the same arguments as in the proof of
[
43
, Theorem 9.21]
.

Lemma 22
.

Let
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
with
\supp\chi\in(-2,2)
and
b\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
with
\supp b\subset T
. Then, for
1<p<\infty
and
1/p<s<1
, there holds

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(f)\|_{s,p,\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\lesssim_{\chi,b,s,p}\|f\|_{s-\frac{1}{p},p,\mathbb{R}^{2}}\qquad\forall f\in W^{s-\frac{1}{p},p}(\mathbb{R}^{2}).

(69)

When
p=2
, the above result is also true for
s=1/2
as the following lemma shows.

Lemma 23
.

Let
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
with
\supp\chi\in(-2,2)
and
b\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
with
\supp b\subset T
. Then, there holds

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(f)\|_{\frac{1}{2},2,\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\lesssim_{\chi,b}\|f\|_{2,\mathbb{R}^{2}}\qquad\forall f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}).

(70)

Proof.

By density, it suffices to consider
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. For
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
define

\displaystyle g_{k}(\bm{x},z):=z^{k}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}b(\bm{y})f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}},\qquad(\bm{x},z)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}.

(71)

Step 1:
H^{1/2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})
bound for
g_{0}
.
Thanks to
eq.

67
, there holds

\displaystyle|g_{0}|_{\frac{1}{2},2,\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}^{2}\approx\int_{0}^{\infty}|g_{0}(\cdot,z)|_{\frac{1}{2},2,\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}{z}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|g_{0}(\bm{x},\cdot)|_{\frac{1}{2},2,\mathbb{R}_{+}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}=:I_{1}+I_{2}.

We now follow the steps in the proof of
[
17
, Theorem 2.2]
. Let
\hat{\cdot}
denote the Fourier transform with respect to the
\bm{x}
-variable. Then,

\displaystyle I_{1}\approx\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\bm{\xi}|\cdot|\hat{g}_{0}(\bm{\xi},z)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{\xi}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\bm{\xi}|\cdot|\hat{b}(\bm{\xi}z)\hat{f}(\bm{\xi})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{\xi}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\bm{\xi}|\cdot\|\hat{b}(\bm{\xi}\cdot)\|_{2,\mathbb{R}_{+}}^{2}\right)|\hat{f}(\bm{\xi})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{\xi}},

where we used the following convolution identity for
z>0
:

\displaystyle g_{0}(\bm{x},z)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}z^{-2}b\left(\frac{\bm{y}-\bm{x}}{z}\right)f(\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}\implies\hat{g}_{0}(\bm{\xi},z)=\hat{b}(\bm{\xi}z)\hat{f}(\bm{\xi}).

(72)

Similarly, there holds

\displaystyle I_{2}\approx\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\hat{g}(\bm{\xi},\cdot)|^{2}_{\frac{1}{2},2,\mathbb{R}_{+}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{\xi}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\hat{b}(\bm{\xi}\cdot)|_{\frac{1}{2},2,\mathbb{R}_{+}}^{2}|\hat{f}(\bm{\xi})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{\xi}}.

Thanks to a change of variables, we obtain

\displaystyle|\bm{\xi}|\cdot\|\hat{b}(\bm{\xi}\cdot)\|_{2,\mathbb{R}_{+}}^{2}+|\hat{b}(\bm{\xi}\cdot)|_{\frac{1}{2},2,\mathbb{R}_{+}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\bm{\omega}\in\mathbb{S}^{2}}\left(|\bm{\xi}|\cdot\|\hat{b}(|\bm{\xi}|\bm{\omega}\cdot)\|_{2,\mathbb{R}_{+}}^{2}+|\hat{b}(|\bm{\xi}|\bm{\omega}\cdot)|_{\frac{1}{2},2,\mathbb{R}_{+}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\bm{\omega}\in\mathbb{S}^{2}}\|\hat{b}(\bm{\omega}\cdot)\|_{\frac{1}{2},2,\mathbb{R}_{+}}^{2},

which is finite since
\hat{b}
is a Schwartz function, and so
|g_{0}|_{\frac{1}{2},2,\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\lesssim_{b}\|f\|_{2,\mathbb{R}^{2}}.

Step 2:
H^{1/2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})
bound on
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(f)
.
For
i=1,2
, there holds

\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\frac{|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(f)(\bm{x}+t\mathbf{e}_{i},z)-\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(f)(\bm{x},z)|^{2}}{t^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\\
=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}|\chi(z)|^{2}\frac{|g_{0}(\bm{x}+t\mathbf{e}_{i},z)-g_{0}(\bm{x},z)|^{2}}{t^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\lesssim_{\chi,b}\|f\|_{2,\mathbb{R}^{2}}.

where we used
eq.

67
and step 1. Thanks to the relation

|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(f)(\bm{x},z+t)-\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(f)(\bm{x},z)|^{2}\lesssim|\chi(z+t)|^{2}|g_{0}(\bm{x},z+t)-g_{0}(\bm{x},t)|^{2}\\
+|\chi(z+t)-\chi(z)|^{2}|g_{0}(\bm{x},z)|^{2},

we obtain

\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\frac{|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(f)(\bm{x},z+t)-\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(f)(\bm{x},z)|^{2}}{t^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\\
\lesssim\|\chi\|_{\infty,\mathbb{R}_{+}}^{2}|g_{0}|_{\frac{1}{2},2,\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\frac{|\chi(z+t)-\chi(z)|^{2}}{t^{2}}|g_{0}(\bm{x},z)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{t}.

Now, applying Hardy’s inequality
[
39
, Theorem 327]
gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{|\chi(z+t)-\chi(z)|^{2}}{t^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{t}\int_{z}^{z+t}\chi^{\prime}(r)\,\mathrm{d}{r}\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{t}\int_{0}^{t}\chi^{\prime}(r+z)\,\mathrm{d}{r}\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\lesssim\|\chi^{\prime}(\cdot+z)\|_{2,\mathbb{R}_{+}}^{2},

and so

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\frac{|\chi(z+t)-\chi(z)|^{2}}{t^{2}}|g_{0}(\bm{x},z)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\|\chi^{\prime}(\cdot+z)\|_{2,\mathbb{R}_{+}}^{2}|g_{0}(\bm{x},z)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}

\displaystyle\leq\|\chi^{\prime}\|_{2,\mathbb{R}_{+}}^{2}\int_{0}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|g_{0}(\bm{x},z)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}.

Applying Young’s inequality to the convolution form of
g_{0}
,
eq.

72
then gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|g_{0}(\bm{x},z)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\leq 2\|b\|_{1,\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}\|f\|_{2,\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}.

Inequality
eq.

70
now follows on collecting results and applying
eq.

67
.

We shall also need the stability of the lifting of the derivative of a smooth function.

Lemma 24
.

Let
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and
b\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. For
1<p<\infty
, there holds

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(\partial_{i}f)\|_{p,\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\lesssim_{\chi,b,p}\|f\|_{1-\frac{1}{p},p,\mathbb{R}^{2}}\qquad\forall f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}).

(73)

Proof.

Let
1<p<\infty
,
f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, and
i\in\{1,2\}
. Integrating by parts gives

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(\partial_{i}f)(\bm{x},z)=\chi(z)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}b(\bm{y})(\partial_{i}f)(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\chi(z)}{z}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}b(\bm{y})\partial_{y_{i}}\{f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\chi(z)}{z}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{i}b)(\bm{y})f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}.

Since
b\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, there holds
\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\partial_{i}b)(\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{y_{i}}=0
, and so

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(\partial_{i}f)(\bm{x},z)

\displaystyle=\chi(z)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{i}b)(\bm{y})\frac{f(\bm{x}+z(\bm{y}-y_{i}\bm{e}_{i}))-f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}.

Applying Hölder’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(\partial_{i}f)(\bm{x},z)|^{p}\lesssim_{\chi,b,p}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|y_{i}(\partial_{i}b)(\bm{y})\right|\left|\frac{f(\bm{x}+z(\bm{y}-y_{i}\bm{e}_{i}))-f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})}{y_{i}z}\right|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}.

Integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}
then gives

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(\partial_{i}f)\|_{p,\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\chi,b,p}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{5}_{+}}\left|y_{i}(\partial_{i}b)(\bm{y})\right|\left|\frac{f(\bm{x}+z(\bm{y}-y_{i}\bm{e}_{i}))-f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})}{y_{i}z}\right|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}

\displaystyle\kern-28.84068pt\mathrel{\mathop{=}\limits^{\tilde{x}_{j}=x_{j}+(1-\delta_{ij})y_{j}z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{5}_{+}}\left|y_{i}(\partial_{i}b)(\bm{y})\right|\left|\frac{f(\tilde{\bm{x}})-f(\tilde{\bm{x}}+y_{i}z\bm{e}_{i})}{y_{i}z}\right|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}\,\mathrm{d}{\tilde{\bm{x}}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}

\displaystyle\kern-6.73212pt\mathrel{\mathop{\lesssim}\limits^{t=y_{i}z}}\|\partial_{i}b\|_{1,\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\frac{|f(\tilde{\bm{x}})-f(\tilde{\bm{x}}+t\bm{e}_{i})|^{p}}{t^{p}}\,\mathrm{d}{\tilde{\bm{x}}}\,\mathrm{d}{t}.

Inequality
eq.

73
now follows from summing over
i
and applying
eq.

67
.

4.2
Continuity of
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}

We now show how the continuity of the operator
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}
can be used to deduce the continuity of
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}
for
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. We begin with a partial result.

Lemma 25
.

Let
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and
b\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
be as in
theorem

21
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
be given. Then, for
1<p<\infty
, there holds

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}(f)\|_{p,\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\lesssim_{\chi,b,k,p}\|f\|_{p,\mathbb{R}^{2}}\qquad\forall f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),

(74)

and for
1/p<s<1
or
(s,p)=(\frac{1}{2},2)
, there holds

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}(f)\|_{s,p,\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\lesssim_{\chi,b,k,s,p}\|f\|_{s-\frac{1}{p},p,\mathbb{R}^{2}}\qquad\forall f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2}).

(75)

Proof.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
1<p<\infty
, and
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. Since the function
\tilde{\chi}:=z^{k}\chi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
with
\supp\tilde{\chi}=\supp\chi
, we have
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}[\chi,b](f)=\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}[\tilde{\chi},b](f)
. Consequently, it suffices to prove
eqs.

74
and
75
in the case
k=0
. To this end, we apply Jensen’s inequality to the identity
eq.

72
to obtain

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(f)\|_{p,\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\|f\|_{p,\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\chi(z)|^{p}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|z^{-2}b\left(\frac{\bm{x}}{z}\right)\right|\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\right)^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{z}

\displaystyle=\|f\|_{p,\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{p}\|b\|_{1,\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{p}\|\chi(z)\|_{1,\mathbb{R}}^{p},

and
eq.

74
follows. Inequality
eq.

75
for
1/p<s<1
is an immediate consequence of
eq.

69
, while the case
(s,p)=(\frac{1}{2},2)
follows from
lemma

23
.

For more precise results, we shall show the effect of taking partial derivatives of
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}(f)
on the index
k
and on the function
f
. To this end, we recall an integration-by-parts formula for tensors. Given two
d
-dimensional tensors
S
and
T
, let
S:T
denote the usual tensor contraction

\displaystyle S:T:=S_{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{d}}T_{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{d}},

where we are using Einstein summation notation. Given a
d
-dimensional tensor
S
with
d\geq 0
and
k\geq 0
, let
D^{k}S
denote the
k
-th derivative tensor of
S
:

\displaystyle(D^{k}S)_{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{d+k}}:=\partial_{i_{d+1}}\partial_{i_{d+2}}\cdots\partial_{i_{d+k}}S_{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{d}},

and let
\dive S
denote the
(d-1)
-dimensional tensor given by

\displaystyle(\dive S)_{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{d-1}}:=\partial_{j}S_{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{d-1}j},

while
\dive^{k}S
,
0\leq k\leq d
, denotes
k
applications of
\dive
to
S
. With this notation, we have the following integration by parts formula for symmetric, smooth, compactly supported tensors
S
and
T
of dimension
d
and
0\leq k\leq d
, respectively:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}S:D^{d-k}T\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}=(-1)^{d-k}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\dive^{d-k}S:T\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}.

With this notation in hand, we have the following identity that shows that the derivatives of
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}(f)
are linear combinations of liftings of derivatives of
f
.

Lemma 26
.

Let
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
,
b\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
be given.
For all
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{3}
and
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, there holds

D^{\alpha}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}(f)(\bm{x},z)\\
=\sum_{i=0}^{\alpha_{3}}\chi_{i}(z)z^{\max\{k+i-|\alpha|,0\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}B_{ki\alpha}(\bm{y}):(D^{\max\{|\alpha|-k-i,0\}}f)(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}

(76)

for suitable
\chi_{i}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
and
\max\{|\alpha|-i-k,0\}
-dimensional tensors
B_{ki\alpha}
with entries in
C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.

Proof.

Let
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and let
g_{k}
be defined as in
eq.

71
. For integers
m\geq k
, there holds

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{m}g_{k}(\bm{x},z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{k}c_{kmj}z^{k-j}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}b(\bm{y})(D^{m-j}f)(\bm{x}+z\bm{y}):\bm{y}^{\otimes m-j}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{k}c_{kmj}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(b(\bm{y})\bm{y}^{\otimes m-j}):D^{k-j}_{\bm{y}}\{(D^{m-k}f)(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left\{\sum_{j=0}^{k}(-1)^{k-j}c_{kmj}\dive^{k-j}(b(\bm{y})\bm{y}^{\otimes m-j})\right\}:(D^{m-k}f)(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle=:\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}B_{km}(\bm{y}):(D^{m-k}f)(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}},

where
c_{kmj}
are suitable constants,
\bm{y}^{\otimes n}
is the tensor product of
n
copies of
\bm{y}
, and
D_{\bm{y}}
denotes the derivative operator with respect to
\bm{y}
. For
0\leq m<k
, there holds

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{m}g_{k}(\bm{x},z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{m}c_{kmj}z^{k-j}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}b(\bm{y})(D^{m-j}f)(\bm{x}+z\bm{y}):\bm{y}^{\otimes m-j}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{m}c_{kmj}z^{k-m}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(b(\bm{y})\bm{y}^{\otimes m-j}):D^{m-j}_{\bm{y}}\{f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle=z^{k-m}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left\{-\sum_{j=0}^{m}(-1)^{m-j}c_{kmj}\dive^{m-j}(b(\bm{y})\bm{y}^{\otimes m-j})\right\}:f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle=:z^{k-m}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}B_{km}(\bm{y}):f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}.

Consequently, there holds

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{m}g_{k}(\bm{x},z)

\displaystyle=z^{\max\{k-m,0\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}B_{km}(\bm{y}):(D^{\max\{m-k,0\}}f)(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}\qquad\forall m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

Now let
\beta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{2}
with
|\beta|\geq k
. Then, there holds

D_{\bm{x}}^{\beta}g_{k}(\bm{x},z)=z^{k}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}b(\bm{y})(D^{\beta}f)(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}b(\bm{y})D^{\tilde{\beta}}_{\bm{y}}\{(D^{\beta-\tilde{\beta}}f)(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}\\
=(-1)^{|\tilde{\beta}|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(D^{\tilde{\beta}}b)(\bm{y})(D^{\beta-\tilde{\beta}}f)(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}=:\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}{B}_{k\beta}(\bm{y})(D^{\beta-\tilde{\beta}}f)(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}},

where
D_{\bm{x}}^{\beta}:=\partial_{x_{1}}^{\beta_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}^{\beta_{2}}
and
\tilde{\beta}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{2}
is any fixed multi-index such that
|\tilde{\beta}|=k
and
\beta-\tilde{\beta}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{2}
. Similar arguments show that for
\beta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{2}
with
|\beta|<k
, there holds

\displaystyle D_{\bm{x}}^{\beta}g_{k}(\bm{x},z)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|\beta|}z^{k-|\beta|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(D^{\beta}b)(\bm{y})f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle=:z^{k-|\beta|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}{B}_{k\beta}(\bm{y})f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}.

Collecting results, for any
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{3}
, there holds

\displaystyle D^{\alpha}g_{k}(\bm{x},z)=z^{\max\{k-|\alpha|,0\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}B_{k\alpha}(\bm{y}):(D^{\max\{|\alpha|-k,0\}}f)(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}

for suitable
\max\{|\alpha|-k,0\}
-dimensional tensors
B_{k\alpha}
with entries in
C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. Equality
eq.

76
now follows from the product rule.

4.3
Proof of
theorem

21

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
1<p<\infty
, and
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. For
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{3}
,
eq.

76
gives

\displaystyle\|D^{\alpha}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}(f)\|_{\sigma,p,\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq i\leq\alpha_{3}\\
\beta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{2}\\
|\beta|=\max\{|\alpha|-k-i,0\}\end{subarray}}\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\max\{k+i-|\alpha|,0\}}[\chi_{i},b_{ki\beta}](D^{\beta}f)\|_{\sigma,p,\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}},

(77)

where
\chi_{i}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and
b_{ki\beta}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
are suitable functions depending on
\chi
and
b
respectively and
0\leq\sigma<1
.

Step 1:
L^{p}
bounds on derivatives.
For
k+i-|\alpha|\geq 0
(so that
|\beta|=0
),
eq.

74
gives

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\max\{k+i-|\alpha|,0\}}[\chi_{i},b_{ki\beta}](D^{\beta}f)\|_{p,\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}=\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k+i-|\alpha|}[\chi_{i},b_{ki\beta}](f)\|_{p,\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\lesssim_{\chi,b,k,p}\|f\|_{p,\mathbb{R}^{2}}.

For
k+i-|\alpha|<0
(so that
|\alpha|\geq k
and
|\beta|\geq 1
), there exists
j\in\{1,2\}
such that
\beta_{j}\geq 1
, and so we apply
eq.

73
to obtain

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}[\chi_{i},b_{ki\beta}](D^{\beta}f)\|_{p,\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}=\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}[\chi_{i},b_{ki\beta}](\partial_{j}D^{\beta-\mathbf{e}_{j}}f)\|_{p,\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\chi,b,k,p}\|D^{\beta-\mathbf{e}_{j}}f\|_{1-\frac{1}{p},p,\mathbb{R}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|f\|_{|\alpha|-k-i-\frac{1}{p},p,\mathbb{R}^{2}}.

Consequently, for all
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, there holds

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}(f)\|_{m,p,\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\lesssim_{\chi,b,k,m,p}\|f\|_{m-k-\frac{1}{p},p,\mathbb{R}^{2}},\qquad m\in\{k+1,k+2,\ldots\}.

(78)

By density,
eq.

78
holds for all
f\in W^{m-k-1/p,p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.

Step 2: The case
s\geq k+1
.
Inequality
eq.

68
for real
s\geq k+1
with
(s,p)\in\mathcal{A}_{k}
follows from
eq.

78
using a standard interpolation argument.

Step 3: The case
k+1/p\leq s<k+1
.
For
s=k+\sigma
, where
1/p<\sigma<1
or
(\sigma,p)=(1/2,2)
, we take
|\alpha|=k
in
eq.

77
and apply
eq.

75
to obtain

\displaystyle|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}(f)|_{s,p,\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\leq\sum_{0\leq i\leq\alpha_{3}}\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i}[\chi_{i},b_{ki\beta}](f)\|_{\sigma,p,\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\lesssim_{\chi,b,k,p,s}\|f\|_{\sigma-\frac{1}{p},p,\mathbb{R}^{2}},

which completes the proof.
\proofbox

5
Weighted
L^{p}
continuity of whole-space operators

In the previous section in
theorem

21
, we established that the lifting operators
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}
are continuous from
W^{s-k-1/p,p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
to
W^{s,p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})
provided that
s>k+1/p
. We now turn to the stability of the operator
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}
with respect to lower-order Sobolev spaces. In particular, we seek to obtain bounds on
\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}(f)\|_{s,p,\mathcal{O}_{1}}
for
0\leq s<k+1/p
, where
\mathcal{O}_{1}:=(0,\infty)^{3}\supset K
is the first octant. It turns out that one suitable space for the lifted function
f
is a weighted
L^{p}
space. Let
\mathcal{Q}_{1}=(0,\infty)^{2}\supset T
denote the first quadrant and let
\rho\in L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}_{1})
be a weight function that satisfying
\rho>0
almost everywhere. Then, for
1<p<\infty
, define

\displaystyle L^{p}(\mathcal{Q}_{1};\rho\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}):=\left\{f\text{ measurable}:\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}|f(\bm{x})|^{p}\rho(\bm{x})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}<\infty\right\}.

(79)

The weights that will appear in our estimates are powers of
\omega_{1}
and
\omega_{2}
defined in
eq.

27
. In particular, the main result of this section is as follows.

Theorem 27
.

Let
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and
b\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
be as in
theorem

21
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
be given. For
1<p<\infty
and
0\leq s<k+1/p
, there holds

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}(f)\|_{s,p,\mathcal{O}_{1}}\lesssim_{\chi,b,k,s,p}\|\omega_{1}^{\frac{1}{p}+k-s}f\|_{p,\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\qquad\forall f\in L^{p}(\mathcal{Q}_{1},\omega_{1}^{1+(k-s)p}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}).

(80)

The proof proceeds in several steps and appears in
section

5.3
.

5.1
Auxiliary results

We begin by recording a number of technical lemmas. Throughout the rest of the section we use the notation
\fint_{\mathcal{O}}fd{\bm{x}}:=|\mathcal{O}|^{-1}\int_{\mathcal{O}}fd{\bm{x}}
.

Lemma 28
.

For
1\leq p<\infty
and
0<h<\infty
, there holds

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left|\fint_{x}^{x+h}f(y)\,\mathrm{d}{y}\right|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{x}\leq h^{p-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}|f(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{x}\qquad\forall f\text{ measurable}.

(81)

Proof.

The result follows on applying Hölder’s inequality and changing the order of integration:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left|\frac{1}{h}\int_{x}^{x+h}f(y)\,\mathrm{d}{y}\right|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{x}

\displaystyle\leq h^{p-2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{x}^{x+h}|f(y)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{y}

\displaystyle=h^{p-2}\left(\int_{0}^{h}\int_{0}^{y}+\int_{h}^{\infty}\int_{y-h}^{y}\right)|f(y)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{x}\,\mathrm{d}{y}.

Lemma 29
.

Let
1<p<\infty
,
0<s<1
, and
0\leq a\leq\infty
. Then, there holds

\displaystyle\int_{(0,a)^{2}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{p}}{|x-y|^{1+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}{x}\,\mathrm{d}{y}\lesssim_{s,p}\int_{(0,a)}x^{(1-s)p}|f^{\prime}(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{x}\qquad\forall f\in W^{1,p}_{\mathrm{loc}}((0,a)).

(82)

Proof.

The proof follows the same arguments as those used in the proof of
[
43
, Theorem 1.28]
, which considers the case
a=\infty
. The full details are given below.

By symmetry, there holds

\displaystyle\int_{(0,a)^{2}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{p}}{|x-y|^{1+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}{x}\,\mathrm{d}{y}

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{a}\int_{y}^{a}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{p}}{(x-y)^{1+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}{x}\,\mathrm{d}{y}

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{a}\int_{y}^{a}\frac{1}{(x-y)^{1+sp}}\left|\int_{y}^{x}f^{\prime}(t)\,\mathrm{d}{t}\right|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{x}\,\mathrm{d}{y}.

Performing a change of variable and applying Hardy’s inequality
[
43
, Theorem 1.3]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{y}^{a}\frac{1}{(x-y)^{1+sp}}\left|\int_{y}^{x}f^{\prime}(t)\,\mathrm{d}{t}\right|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{x}

\displaystyle\kern-7.9763pt\mathrel{\mathop{\leq}\limits^{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{x}=x-y\\
\tau=t-y\end{subarray}}}\int_{0}^{a-y}\frac{1}{\tilde{x}^{1+sp}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tilde{x}}|\tau f^{\prime}(y+\tau)|\frac{\,\mathrm{d}{\tau}}{\tau}\right)^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\tilde{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{s^{p}}\int_{0}^{a-y}\frac{|f^{\prime}(y+\tilde{x})|^{p}}{\tilde{x}^{1+(s-1)p}}\,\mathrm{d}{\tilde{x}}

\displaystyle\kern-9.69853pt\mathrel{\mathop{=}\limits^{x=\tilde{x}+y}}\frac{1}{s^{p}}\int_{y}^{a}\frac{|f^{\prime}(x)|^{p}}{(x-y)^{1+(s-1)p}}\,\mathrm{d}{x}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\int_{(0,a)^{2}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{p}}{|x-y|^{1+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}{x}\,\mathrm{d}{y}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{s^{p}}\int_{0}^{a}\int_{y}^{a}\frac{|f^{\prime}(x)|^{p}}{(x-y)^{1+(s-1)p}}\,\mathrm{d}{x}\,\mathrm{d}{y}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{s^{p}}\int_{0}^{a}|f^{\prime}(x)|^{p}\int_{0}^{x}\frac{1}{(x-y)^{1+(s-1)p}}\,\mathrm{d}{y}\,\mathrm{d}{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{s^{p}(1-s)p}\int_{0}^{a}x^{(1-s)p}|f^{\prime}(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{x},

which completes the proof.

Lemma 30
.

Let
1<p<\infty
and
0<s<1/p
. For all
f\in L^{p}(\mathcal{Q}_{1};\omega_{1}^{1-sp}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})
, there holds

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\int_{0}^{2}\int_{x_{1}}^{x_{1}+z}\int_{x_{2}}^{x_{2}+z}\frac{1}{z^{2+sp}}|f(\bm{y})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{y_{2}}\,\mathrm{d}{y_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\lesssim_{s,p}\|\omega_{1}^{\frac{1}{p}-s}f\|_{p,\mathcal{Q}_{1}}^{p}.

(83)

Proof.

Applying
eq.

81
and using that
0<z<2
gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\fint_{x_{2}}^{x_{2}+z}\left(\int_{x_{1}}^{x_{1}+z}|f(\bm{y})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{y_{1}}\right)\,\mathrm{d}{y_{2}}\,\mathrm{d}{x_{2}}\lesssim_{p}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{x_{1}}^{x_{1}+z}|f(y_{1},x_{2})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{y_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}{x_{2}}.

Moreover, there holds

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{2}\int_{x_{1}}^{x_{1}+z}\frac{1}{z^{1+sp}}|f(y_{1},x_{2})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{y_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{x_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{x_{1}}^{x_{1}+2}|f(y_{1},x_{2})|^{p}\int_{y_{1}-x_{1}}^{2}\frac{1}{z^{1+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{y_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}{x_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim_{s,p}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{x_{1}}^{x_{1}+2}(y_{1}-x_{1})^{-sp}|f(y_{1},x_{2})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{y_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}{x_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad=\left(\int_{0}^{2}\int_{0}^{y_{1}}+\int_{2}^{\infty}\int_{y_{1}-2}^{y_{1}}\right)(y_{1}-x_{1})^{-sp}|f(y_{1},x_{2})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{x_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}{y_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim_{s,p}\int_{0}^{\infty}\min\{y_{1},2\}^{1-sp}|f(y_{1},x_{2})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{y_{1}}.

The result now follows on integrating over
0<x_{2}<\infty
.

Lemma 31
.

Let
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and
b\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
be as in
theorem

21
and
1<p<\infty
. Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
f\in L^{p}(\mathcal{Q}_{1};\omega_{1}^{1+kp}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})
. For
0<t<2
, there holds

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}|g_{k}(\bm{x},z)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\lesssim_{b,k,p}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\min\{x_{1},t\}^{1+kp}|f(\bm{x})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}},

(84)

where
g_{k}
is defined in
eq.

71

Proof.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
1<p<\infty
, and
f\in L^{p}(\mathcal{Q}_{1};\omega_{1}^{1+kp}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})
be given. Let
z\in(0,t)
. Then, for
\bm{x}\in\mathcal{Q}_{1}
and
\bm{y}\in(0,1)^{2}
, there holds
z\leq\min\{x_{1}+zy_{1},t\}/y_{1}
, and so

\displaystyle|g_{k}(\bm{x},z)|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{(0,1)^{2}}\min\{x_{1}+zy_{1},t\}^{k}|y_{1}^{-k}b(\bm{y})||f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})|\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle\kern-6.08923pt\mathrel{\mathop{\lesssim_{b,k}}\limits^{\bm{u}=\bm{x}+z\bm{y}}}\fint_{x_{2}}^{x_{2}+z}\fint_{x_{1}}^{x_{1}+z}\min\{u_{1},t\}^{k}|f(\bm{u})|\,\mathrm{d}{u_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}{u_{2}}.

Integrating over
x_{2}\in(0,\infty)
and applying
eq.

81
to the function

\displaystyle\tilde{f}(u_{2};x_{1},z)=\fint_{x_{1}}^{x_{1}+z}|\tilde{\omega}_{1}^{k}f(\bm{u})|\,\mathrm{d}{u_{1}},\qquad\text{where }\tilde{\omega}_{1}(\bm{u}):=\min\{u_{1},t\}^{k}

and using that
0<z<t<2
gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}|g_{k}(\bm{x},z)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{x_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\fint_{x_{2}}^{x_{2}+z}\tilde{f}(u_{2};x_{1},z)\,\mathrm{d}{u_{2}}\right)^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{x_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\fint_{x_{1}}^{x_{1}+z}|(\tilde{\omega}_{1}^{k}f)(u_{1},x_{2})|\,\mathrm{d}{u_{1}}\right)^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{x_{2}}.

Hardy’s inequality
[
39
, Theorem 327]
then shows that, for
0<x_{2}<\infty
, there holds

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left(\fint_{x_{1}}^{x_{1}+z}|(\tilde{\omega}_{1}^{k}f)(u_{1},x_{2})|\,\mathrm{d}{u_{1}}\right)^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\quad

\displaystyle\kern-10.89822pt\mathrel{\mathop{=}\limits^{v=x_{1}+z}}\int_{x_{1}}^{x_{1}+t}\left(\fint_{x_{1}}^{v}|(\tilde{\omega}_{1}^{k}f)(u_{1},x_{2})|\,\mathrm{d}{u_{1}}\right)^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{v}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{p}\int_{x_{1}}^{x_{1}+t}|(\tilde{\omega}_{1}^{k}f)(v,x_{2})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{v},

and so

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}|g_{k}(\bm{x},z)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{x_{2}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\lesssim_{b,k,p}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{x_{1}}^{x_{1}+t}|(\tilde{\omega}_{1}^{k}f)(v,x_{2})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{v}\,\mathrm{d}{x_{2}}.

Integrating over
x_{1}
and changing the order of integration gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}|g_{k}(\bm{x},z)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{b,k,p}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\int_{x_{1}}^{x_{1}+t}|(\tilde{\omega}_{1}^{k}f)(v,x_{2})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{v}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{v}+\int_{t}^{\infty}\int_{v-t}^{v}\right)|(\tilde{\omega}_{1}^{k}f)(v,x_{2})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{x_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}{v}\,\mathrm{d}{x_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\tilde{\omega}_{1}(v,x_{2})^{1+kp}|f(v,x_{2})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{v}\,\mathrm{d}{x_{2}},

which completes the proof.

5.2
Continuity of
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}

In this section, we prove
theorem

27
in the case
k=0
. We will utilize the following equivalent norm on
W^{s,p}(\mathcal{O}_{1})
.

Lemma 32
.

For all
p\in(1,\infty)
,
s\in(0,1)
, and
f\in W^{s,p}(\mathcal{O}_{1})
, there holds

\displaystyle\|f\|_{s,p,\mathcal{O}_{1}}^{p}\approx_{s,p}\|f\|_{p,\mathcal{O}_{1}}^{p}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{1}}\frac{|f(\bm{x}+t\mathbf{e}_{i})-f(\bm{x})|^{p}}{t^{1+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{t}.

(85)

Proof.

Let
f\in W^{s,p}(\mathcal{O}_{1})
. Thanks to
[
43
, Theorem 6.38]
, there holds

\displaystyle|f|_{s,p,\mathcal{O}_{1}}^{p}\approx_{s,p}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{1}}\frac{|f(\bm{x}+t\mathbf{e}_{i})-f(\bm{x})|^{p}}{t^{1+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{t},

and
eq.

85
now follows on noting that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\int_{1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{1}}\frac{|f(\bm{x}+t\mathbf{e}_{i})-f(\bm{x})|^{p}}{t^{1+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\lesssim_{s,p}\|f\|_{p,\mathcal{O}_{1}}^{p}.

We now estimate each term in
eq.

85
. The first result deals with terms involving translations in the first two coordinate directions.

Lemma 33
.

Let
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and
b\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
be as in
theorem

21
.
For
1<p<\infty
,
0<s<1/p
, and
1\leq i\leq 2
, there holds

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{1}}\frac{|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(f)(\bm{x}+t\bm{e}_{i},z)-\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(f)(\bm{x},z)|^{p}}{t^{1+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\lesssim_{\chi,b,s,p}\|\omega_{1}^{\frac{1}{p}-s}f\|_{p,\mathcal{Q}_{1}}^{p}

(86)

for all
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathcal{Q}_{1})
.

Proof.

Let
1<p<\infty
,
0<s<1/p
, and
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathcal{Q}_{1})
be given. Let
g_{0}(\cdot,\cdot)
be as in
eq.

71
with
k=0
and let
\tilde{g}(\bm{x},z,t):=g_{0}(\bm{x}+t\mathbf{e}_{i},z)-g_{0}(\bm{x},z)
.

Step 1.
Let
1\leq i\leq 2
. We will show that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\frac{|\tilde{g}(\bm{x},z,t)|^{p}}{t^{1+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\lesssim_{b,s,p}\|\omega_{1}^{1/p-s}f\|_{p,\mathcal{Q}_{1}}^{p}.

(87)

We begin by decomposing the above integral into two terms:

\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\frac{|\tilde{g}(\bm{x},z,t)|^{p}}{t^{1+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\\
=\left(\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}+\int_{0}^{1}\int_{t}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\right)\frac{|\tilde{g}(\bm{x},z,t)|^{p}}{t^{1+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{t}=:A_{i}+B_{i}.

Part (a):
A_{i}
.
Let
0<t<1
. Then,
f(\cdot+t\mathbf{e}_{i})-f(\cdot)\in L^{p}(\mathcal{Q}_{1},\omega_{1}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})
and

\displaystyle\tilde{g}(\bm{x},z,t)=\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}b(\bm{y})\left[f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y}+t\mathbf{e}_{i})-f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})\right]\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}.

Integrating
eq.

84
over
0<t<1
then gives

\displaystyle A_{i}\lesssim_{b,p}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}\min\{x_{1},t\}\frac{|f(\bm{x}+t\mathbf{e}_{i})|^{p}+|f(\bm{x})|^{p}}{t^{1+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}.

For
i=1
, there holds

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{1}t^{-sp}|f(\bm{x}+t\bm{e}_{1})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\,\mathrm{d}{x_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad\kern-12.95842pt\mathrel{\mathop{=}\limits^{\tilde{x}_{1}=x_{1}+t}}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{t}^{\infty}t^{-sp}|f(\tilde{x}_{1},x_{2})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\tilde{x}_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}{t}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\tilde{x}_{1}}+\int_{1}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{1}\right)t^{-sp}|f(\tilde{x}_{1},x_{2})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\,\mathrm{d}{\tilde{x}_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,p}\int_{0}^{\infty}\min\{\tilde{x}_{1},1\}^{1-sp}|f(\tilde{x}_{1},x_{2})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\tilde{x}_{1}}.

On the other hand, note that for any
0\leq u<\infty
and
\tilde{f}(\bm{x})=f(\bm{x}+u\mathbf{e}_{2})
, there holds

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{1}\min\{x_{1},t\}t^{-(1+sp)}|\tilde{f}(\bm{x})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\,\mathrm{d}{x_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad=\left(\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{x_{1}}+\int_{1}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{1}\right)t^{-sp}|\tilde{f}(\bm{x})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\,\mathrm{d}{x_{1}}+\int_{0}^{1}\int_{x_{1}}^{1}t^{-(1+sp)}x_{1}|\tilde{f}(\bm{x})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\,\mathrm{d}{x_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim_{s,p}\int_{0}^{\infty}\min\{x_{1},1\}^{1-sp}|\tilde{f}(\bm{x})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{x_{1}}+\int_{0}^{1}(x_{1}^{-sp}-1)x_{1}|\tilde{f}(\bm{x})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{x_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\min\{x_{1},1\}^{1-sp}|f(x_{1},x_{2}+u)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{x_{1}}.

The bound
A_{i}\lesssim_{b,s,p}\|\omega_{1}^{\frac{1}{p}-s}f\|_{p,\mathcal{Q}_{1}}^{p}
now follows on performing a change of variables and collecting results.

Part (b):
B_{i}
.
Using identity
eq.

72
, we obtain

\displaystyle\tilde{g}(\bm{x},z,t)

\displaystyle=z^{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left[b\left(\frac{\bm{y}-\bm{x}-t\mathbf{e}_{i}}{z}\right)-b\left(\frac{\bm{y}-\bm{x}}{z}\right)\right]f(\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle=-z^{-3}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}(\partial_{i}b)\left(\frac{\bm{y}-\bm{x}-r\mathbf{e}_{i}}{z}\right)f(\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{r}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}.

Writing
z^{-2}|\partial_{i}b|=(z^{-2}|\partial_{i}b|)^{1-1/p}(z^{-2}|\partial_{i}b|)^{1/p}
and applying Hölder’s inequality gives

\displaystyle|\tilde{g}(\bm{x},z,t)|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{1}{z^{2}}|\partial_{i}b|\left(\frac{\bm{y}-\bm{x}-r\mathbf{e}_{i}}{z}\right)\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}\,\mathrm{d}{r}\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\frac{1}{z^{p+2}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\partial_{i}b|\left(\frac{\bm{y}-\bm{x}-r\mathbf{e}_{i}}{z}\right)|f(\bm{y})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}\,\mathrm{d}{r}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{b,p}\frac{t^{p-1}}{z^{p+2}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\partial_{i}b|\left(\frac{\bm{y}-\bm{x}-r\mathbf{e}_{i}}{z}\right)|f(\bm{y})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}\,\mathrm{d}{r}.

Integrating over
\bm{x}
gives

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}|\tilde{g}(\bm{x},z,t)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}

\displaystyle\quad\kern-13.2057pt\mathrel{\mathop{\leq}\limits^{\tilde{\bm{x}}=\bm{x}+r\mathbf{e}_{i}}}\frac{t^{p-1}}{z^{p+2}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\partial_{i}b|\left(\frac{\bm{y}-\tilde{\bm{x}}}{z}\right)|f(\bm{y})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}\,\mathrm{d}{\tilde{\bm{x}}}\,\mathrm{d}{r}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{t^{p}}{z^{p+2}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\partial_{i}b|\left(\frac{\bm{y}-\tilde{\bm{x}}}{z}\right)|f(\bm{y})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}\,\mathrm{d}{\tilde{\bm{x}}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim_{b}\frac{t^{p}}{z^{p+2}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\int_{x_{1}}^{x_{1}+z}\int_{x_{2}}^{x_{2}+z}|f(\bm{y})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{y_{2}}\,\mathrm{d}{y_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}.

Integrating over
z
and
t
, we obtain

\displaystyle B_{i}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{b,p}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{t}^{2}\frac{t^{(1-s)p-1}}{z^{p+2}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\int_{x_{1}}^{x_{1}+z}\int_{x_{2}}^{x_{2}+z}|f(\bm{y})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{y_{2}}\,\mathrm{d}{y_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2}\int_{0}^{z}\frac{t^{(1-s)p-1}}{z^{p+2}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\int_{x_{1}}^{x_{1}+z}\int_{x_{2}}^{x_{2}+z}|f(\bm{y})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{y_{2}}\,\mathrm{d}{y_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\,\mathrm{d}{z}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p(1-s)}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\int_{0}^{2}\int_{x_{1}}^{x_{1}+z}\int_{x_{2}}^{x_{2}+z}\frac{1}{z^{2+sp}}|f(\bm{y})|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{y_{2}}\,\mathrm{d}{y_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}.

Applying
eq.

83
, we obtain
B_{i}\lesssim_{b,s,p}\|\omega_{1}^{\frac{1}{p}-s}f\|_{p,\mathcal{Q}_{1}}^{p}
, which completes the proof of
eq.

87
.

Step 2.
Since
\supp\chi\subset B(0,2)
, there holds

\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{1}}\frac{|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(f)(\bm{x}+t\mathbf{e}_{i},z)-\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(f)(\bm{x},z)|^{p}}{t^{1+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\\
=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}|\chi(z)|^{p}\frac{|\tilde{g}(\bm{x},z,t)|^{p}}{t^{1+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\lesssim_{\chi,b,s,p}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\min\{x_{1},1\}^{1-sp}f(\bm{x})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}},

which completes the proof.

The next result deals with the term involving a translation in the
z
-direction.

Lemma 34
.

Let
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and
b\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
be as in
theorem

21
. For
1<p<\infty
and
0<s<1/p
, there holds

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{1}}\frac{|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(f)(\bm{x},z+t)-\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(f)(\bm{x},z)|^{p}}{t^{1+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\lesssim_{\chi,b,s,p}\|\omega_{1}^{\frac{1}{p}-s}f\|_{p,\mathcal{Q}_{1}}^{p}

(88)

for all
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathcal{Q}_{1})
.

Proof.

Let
1<p<\infty
,
0<s<1/p
, and
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathcal{Q}_{1})
be given. Let
g_{0}(\cdot,\cdot)
be defined as in
eq.

71
.

Step 1.
We will first show that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{Q}}\frac{|g_{0}(\bm{x},z+t)-g_{0}(\bm{x},z)|^{p}}{t^{1+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\lesssim_{b,s,p}\|\omega_{1}^{\frac{1}{p}-s}f\|_{p,\mathcal{Q}_{1}}^{p}.

(89)

Applying
eq.

82
gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{|g_{0}(\bm{x},z+t)-g_{0}(\bm{x},z)|^{p}}{t^{1+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{t}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{2}z^{(1-s)p}|\partial_{z}g_{0}(\bm{x},z)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{z}.

(90)

Applying identity
eq.

72
, we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{z}g_{0}(\bm{x},z)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial_{z}\left\{z^{-2}b\left(\frac{\bm{y}-\bm{x}}{z}\right)\right\}f(\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left\{2z^{-3}b\left(\frac{\bm{y}-\bm{x}}{z}\right)+z^{-4}Db\left(\frac{\bm{y}-\bm{x}}{z}\right)\cdot(\bm{y}-\bm{x})\right\}f(\bm{y})\,\mathrm{d}{y_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}{y_{2}}.

For
\bm{y}\in(x_{1},x_{1}+z)\times(x_{2},x_{2}+z)
, there holds

\displaystyle\left|2z^{-3}b\left(\frac{\bm{y}-\bm{x}}{z}\right)+z^{-4}Db\left(\frac{\bm{y}-\bm{x}}{z}\right)\cdot(\bm{y}-\bm{x})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{b}z^{-3},

where we used that
b
and
Db
are uniformly bounded. Since
\supp b\subset(0,1)^{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2}z^{(1-s)p}|\partial_{z}g_{0}(\bm{x},z)|\,\mathrm{d}{z}\lesssim_{b}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{1}{z^{2+sp}}\int_{x_{2}}^{x_{2}+z}\int_{x_{1}}^{x_{1}+z}|f(\bm{y})|\,\mathrm{d}{y_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}{y_{2}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}.

Inequality
eq.

89
now follows on integrating
eq.

90
over
\bm{x}\in\mathcal{Q}_{1}
and applying
eq.

83
.

Step 2.
For
0<t<2
and
\bm{x}\in\mathcal{Q}_{1}
, we add and subtract
\chi(z+t)g_{0}(\bm{x},t)
to obtain

|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(f)(\bm{x},z+t)-\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(f)(\bm{x},z)|^{p}\lesssim_{p}|\chi(z+t)|^{p}|g_{0}(\bm{x},z+t)-g_{0}(\bm{x},t)|^{p}\\
+|\chi(z+t)-\chi(z)|^{p}|g_{0}(\bm{x},z)|^{p}.

For the first term, we use that
\supp\chi\in(-2,2)
and apply
eq.

89
to obtain

\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{1}}|\chi(z+t)|^{p}\frac{|g_{0}(\bm{x},z+t)-g_{0}(\bm{x},t)|^{p}}{t^{1+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\\
\lesssim_{\chi,p}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\frac{|g_{0}(\bm{x},z+t)-g_{0}(\bm{x},t)|^{p}}{t^{1+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{t}\lesssim_{b,s,p}\|\omega_{1}^{\frac{1}{p}-s}f\|_{p,\mathcal{Q}_{1}}^{p}.

For the second term, we again use that
\supp\chi\in(-2,2)
as well as the assumption
0<s<1/p
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{1}}\frac{|\chi(z+t)-\chi(z)|^{p}}{t^{1+sp}}|g_{0}(\bm{x},z)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{t}

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\frac{1}{t^{1+sp}}\left|\int_{z}^{z+t}\chi^{\prime}(r)\,\mathrm{d}{r}\right|^{p}|g_{0}(\bm{x},z)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{t}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim_{\chi,p}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}t^{(1-s)p-1}|g_{0}(\bm{x},z)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}\,\mathrm{d}{t}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim_{s,p}\int_{0}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}|g_{0}(\bm{x},z)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}\,\mathrm{d}{z}.

Inequality
eq.

88
now follows from
eq.

84
.

We now obtain
theorem

27
in the case
k=0
.

Lemma 35
.

Let
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and
b\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
be as in
theorem

21
. For
1<p<\infty
and
0\leq s<1/p
, there holds

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(f)\|_{s,p,\mathcal{O}_{1}}\lesssim_{\chi,b,k,s,p}\|\omega_{1}^{\frac{1}{p}-s}f\|_{p,\mathcal{Q}_{1}}\qquad\forall f\in L^{p}(\mathcal{Q}_{1},\omega_{1}^{1-sp}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}).

(91)

Proof.

The case
s=0
follows on taking
t=2
in
eq.

84
and using the fact that
\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}(f)\|_{p,\mathcal{O}_{1}}\lesssim_{\chi,p}\|g\|_{p,\mathcal{Q}_{1}\times(0,2)}
, where
g
is defined in
eq.

71
. The case
0<s<1/p
follows from the norm equivalence
eq.

85
, the bounds
eqs.

86
and
88
, and the density of
C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathcal{O}_{1})
in
L^{p}(\mathcal{O}_{1},\omega_{1}^{1-sp}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}})
.

5.3
Proof of
theorem

27

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
1<p<\infty
.

Step 1:
s=0
.
Taking
t=2
in
eq.

84
and using the fact that
\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}(f)\|_{p,\mathcal{O}_{1}}\lesssim_{\chi,p}\|g_{k}\|_{p,\mathcal{Q}_{1}\times(0,2)}
, where
g_{k}
is defined in
eq.

71
, we obtain
eq.

80
in the case
s=0
.

Step 2:
s\in\{1,2,\ldots,k\}
.
Let
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathcal{Q}_{1})
. Applying
eq.

76
with
|\alpha|\leq k
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|D^{\alpha}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}(f)\|_{\sigma,p,\mathcal{O}_{1}}\leq\sum_{0\leq i\leq\alpha_{3}}\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k+i-|\alpha|}[\chi_{i},b_{ki}](f)\|_{\sigma,p,\mathcal{O}_{1}},

(92)

where
\chi_{i}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and
b_{ki}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
are suitable functions depending on
\chi
and
b
respectively and
0\leq\sigma<1
. Applying
eq.

80
with
s=0
then gives

\displaystyle|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}(f)|_{s,p,\mathcal{O}_{1}}\lesssim_{\chi,b,k,s,p}\|\omega_{1}^{\frac{1}{p}+k-s}f\|_{p,\mathcal{Q}_{1}},

where we used that
k+i-|\alpha|\geq k-m
for
0\leq i\leq\alpha_{3}
. By density,
eq.

80
holds for
s\in\{0,1,\ldots,k\}
.

Step 3:
0\leq s\leq k
.
This case follows from interpolating Step 2 (see e.g.
[
22
, Theorem 14.2.3]
and
[
16
, Theorem 5.4.1]
).

Step 4:
k<s<k+1/p
.
Let
\sigma=s-k
so that
0<\sigma<1/p
. Setting
\tilde{\chi}_{ik\alpha}(z):=z^{k+i-|\alpha|}\chi_{i}\in C^{\infty_{c}}(\mathcal{Q}_{1})
so that
\supp\tilde{\chi}_{ik\alpha}\in(-2,2)
and applying
eqs.

92
and
91
then gives

\displaystyle\|D^{\alpha}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}(f)\|_{\sigma,p,\mathcal{O}_{1}}\leq\sum_{0\leq i\leq\alpha_{3}}\|\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{0}[\tilde{\chi}_{ik\alpha},b_{ki}](f)\|_{\sigma,p,\mathcal{O}_{1}}\lesssim_{\chi,b,k,\sigma,p}\|\omega_{1}^{\frac{1}{p}-\sigma}f\|_{p,\mathcal{Q}_{1}}.

Inequality
eq.

80
now follows.
\proofbox

6
Continuity of fundamental operators

In this section, we prove the continuity and interpolation properties of the four fundamental operators
\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}
defined in
eq.

21
,
\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[1]}
defined in
eq.

28
,
\mathcal{S}_{k,r}^{[1]}
defined in
eq.

45
, and
\mathcal{R}_{k,r}^{[1]}
defined in
eq.

57
. We begin with the properties of
\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}
, which rely on the results of
section

4
. Then, in
section

6.2
, we show that the four fundamental operators are continuous from weighted
L^{p}
spaces
eq.

79
to
W^{s,p}(K)
for small
s
, which will be useful for the analysis of
\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[1]}
,
\mathcal{S}_{k,r}^{[1]}
, and
\mathcal{R}_{k,r}^{[1]}
. This section concludes with the proofs of
lemmas

10
,
14
, and
18
.

6.1
Proof of
lemma

8

Step 1: Continuity
eq.

24
.
Let
\tilde{b}
denote the extension by zero of
b
to
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and let
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
with
\chi\equiv 1
on
(-1,1)
and
\supp\chi\in(-2,2)
. Let
f\in W^{s-k-\frac{1}{p},p}(T)
be given and let
\tilde{f}
denote a bounded extension
f
to
\mathbb{R}^{2}
satisfying
\|\tilde{f}\|_{s,p,\mathbb{R}^{2}}\lesssim_{s,p}\|f\|_{s,p,T}
; see e.g.
[
33
]
. Thanks to the identity

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{k}(f)=\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{k}(f)[\chi,\tilde{b}](f)\qquad\text{on }K,

(93)

where
\tilde{E}_{k}
is defined in
eq.

66
, inequality

eq.

24
immediately follows from
eq.

68
and the smoothness of the mapping
\mathfrak{I}_{1}
defined in
eq.

22
.

Step 2: Trace property
eq.

23
.
Direct computation shows that
eq.

23
holds.

Step 3: Polynomial preservation.
If
f\in\mathcal{P}_{N}(\Gamma_{1})
,
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then direct inspection reveals that
\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}(f)\in\mathcal{P}_{N+k}(K)
.
\proofbox

6.2
Weighted continuity

We begin with the continuity of
\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}
.

Lemma 36
.

Let
b\in C_{c}^{\infty}(T)
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
1<p<\infty
, and
0\leq s<k+1/p
or
(s,p)=(k+\frac{1}{2},2)
. Then, for all
t_{1},t_{2},t_{3}\in[0,\infty)
such that
t_{1}+t_{2}+t_{3}=k-s+1/p
, there holds

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}(f)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,s,p}\|\omega_{1}^{t_{1}}\omega_{2}^{t_{2}}\omega_{3}^{t_{3}}f\|_{p,T}\qquad\forall f\in L^{p}(T;(\omega_{1}^{t_{1}}\omega_{2}^{t_{2}}\omega_{3}^{t_{3}})^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}),

(94)

where
\omega_{i}
are defined in
eq.

27
.

Proof.

Let
t=k-s+1/p
.

Step 1:
t_{2}=t_{3}=0
.
Let
\tilde{b}
denote the extension by zero of
b
to
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and let
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
with
\chi\equiv 1
on
(-1,1)
and
\supp\chi\in(-2,2)
. Let
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(T)
be given and let
\tilde{f}
denote the extension by zero of
f
to
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Thanks to the identity
eq.

93
,
eq.

94
with
t_{2}=t_{3}=0
follows from
eq.

80
and a standard density argument. The case
(s,p)=(k+\frac{1}{2},2)
follows from a similar argument using
eq.

68
.

Step 2:
t_{1}=t_{3}=0
.
We define transformations
\mathfrak{F}_{1}:T\to T
and
\mathfrak{G}_{1}:K\to K
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{1}(\bm{x}):=(x_{2},x_{1})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{G}_{1}(\bm{x},z):=(x_{2},x_{1},z)\qquad(\bm{x},z)\in K.

(95)

Then, a change of variable shows that
\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}(f)\circ\mathfrak{G}_{1}=\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}[b\circ\mathfrak{F}_{1}](f\circ\mathfrak{F}_{1})
, and so

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}(f)\|_{s,p,K}=\|\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}(f)\circ\mathfrak{G}_{1}\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,s,p}\|\omega_{1}^{t}(f\circ\mathfrak{F}_{1})\|_{p,T}=\|\omega_{2}^{t}f\|_{p,T},

where we applied Step 1 in the middle inequality.

Step 3:
t_{3}=0
.
Applying Steps 1 and 2 and interpolating between
L^{p}(T;\omega_{1}^{tp}d\bm{x})
and
L^{p}(T;\omega_{2}^{tp}d\bm{x})
(see e.g.
[
16
, Theorem 5.4.1]
) then gives
eq.

94
.

Step 4:
t_{1}=t_{2}=0
.
We define transformations
\mathfrak{F}_{2}:T\to T
and
\mathfrak{G}_{2}:K\to K
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{2}(\bm{x}):=(x_{2},1-x_{1}-x_{2})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{G}_{2}(\bm{x},z):=(1-x_{1}-x_{2}-z,x_{1},z)\qquad(\bm{x},z)\in K.

(96)

A change of variables then gives
\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}(f)\circ\mathfrak{G}_{2}=\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}[b\circ\mathfrak{F}_{2}](f\circ\mathfrak{F}_{2})
,
and so

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}(f)\|_{t,p,K}\lesssim\|\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}(f)\circ\mathfrak{G}_{2}\|_{t,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,t,p}\|\omega_{1}^{t}(f\circ\mathfrak{F}_{2})\|_{p,T}=\|\omega_{3}^{t}f\|_{p,T},

where we applied Step 1 in the middle inequality.

Step 5: General case.
Applying Steps 3 and 4 and interpolating between
L^{p}(T;(\omega_{1}^{r_{1}}\omega_{2}^{r_{2}})^{p}d\bm{x})
with
r_{1},r_{2}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
with
r_{1}+r_{2}=t
and
L^{p}(T;\omega_{3}^{tp}d\bm{x})
(see e.g.
[
16
, Theorem 5.4.1]
) gives

eq.

94
.

We now turn to the continuity of
\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[1]}
.

Lemma 37
.

Let
b\in C_{c}^{\infty}(T)
,
k,r\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
1<p<\infty
, and
0\leq s<k+1/p
or
(s,p)=(k+\frac{1}{2},2)
. Then, for all
t_{1},t_{2},t_{3}\in[0,\infty)
such that
t_{1}+t_{2}+t_{3}=k-s+1/p
, there holds

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,r,s,p}\|\omega_{1}^{t_{1}}\omega_{2}^{t_{2}}\omega_{3}^{t_{3}}f\|_{p,T}\qquad\forall f\in L^{p}(T;(\omega_{1}^{t_{1}}\omega_{2}^{t_{2}}\omega_{3}^{t_{3}})^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}),

(97)

where
\omega_{i}
are defined in
eq.

27
.

Proof.

Let
0\leq s<k+1/p
or
(s,p)=(k+\frac{1}{2},2)
. We proceed by induction on
r
. The case
r=0
follows from
eq.

94
, so assume that
eq.

97
holds for some
r\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Direction computation gives

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{k,r+1}^{[1]}(f)(\bm{x},z)-\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f)(\bm{x},z)

\displaystyle=x_{2}^{r}\frac{(-z)^{k}}{k!}\int_{T}b(\bm{y})\frac{f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})}{(x_{2}+zy_{2})^{r}}\left(\frac{x_{2}}{x_{2}+zy_{2}}-1\right)\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle=x_{2}^{r}\frac{(-z)^{k+1}}{k!}\int_{T}y_{2}b(\bm{y})\frac{f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})}{(x_{2}+zy_{2})^{r+1}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle=(k+1)\mathcal{M}_{k+1,r}^{[1]}[\omega_{2}b](\omega_{2}^{-1}f)(\bm{x},z),

which leads to the following identity

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{k,r+1}^{[1]}(f)=(k+1)\mathcal{M}_{k+1,r}^{[1]}[\omega_{2}b](\omega_{2}^{-1}f)+\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f).

(98)

Consequently, there holds

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{M}_{k,r+1}^{[1]}(f)\|_{s,p,K}

\displaystyle\leq(k+1)\|\mathcal{M}_{k+1,r}^{[1]}[\omega_{2}b](\omega_{2}^{-1}f)\|_{s,p,K}+\|\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[1]}(f)\|_{s,p,K}.

Applying
eq.

97
with
\tau_{1}=t_{1}
,
\tau_{2}=t_{2}+1
and
\tau_{3}=t_{3}
gives

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{M}_{k+1,r}^{[1]}[\omega_{2}b](\omega_{2}^{-1}f)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,r,s,p}\|\omega_{1}^{\tau_{1}}\omega_{2}^{\tau_{2}-1}\omega_{3}^{\tau_{3}}f\|_{p,T}=\|\omega_{1}^{t_{1}}\omega_{2}^{t_{2}}\omega_{3}^{t_{3}}f\|_{p,T},

and so
\|\mathcal{M}_{k,r+1}^{[1]}(f)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,r,s,p}\|\omega_{1}^{t_{1}}\omega_{2}^{t_{2}}\omega_{3}^{t_{3}}f\|_{p,T}
, which completes the proof.

It will be convenient to define a three-parameter version of
\mathcal{S}_{k,r}^{[1]}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{k,r,q}^{[1]}(f)(\bm{x},z)

\displaystyle:=x_{1}^{q}x_{2}^{r}\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}(\omega_{1}^{-q}\omega_{2}^{-r}f)(\bm{x},z)

(99)

for
k,r,q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. This three-parameter version satisfies the same continuity properties as
\mathcal{E}_{k}^{[1]}
and
\mathcal{M}_{k,r}^{[1]}
.

Lemma 38
.

Let
b\in C_{c}^{\infty}(T)
,
k,r,q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
1<p<\infty
, and
0\leq s<k+1/p
or
(s,p)=(k+\frac{1}{2},2)
. Then, for all
t_{1},t_{2},t_{3}\in[0,\infty)
such that
t_{1}+t_{2}+t_{3}=k-s+1/p
, there holds

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}_{k,r,q}^{[1]}(f)\|_{s,p,K}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{b,k,r,q,s,p}\|\omega_{1}^{t_{1}}\omega_{2}^{t_{2}}\omega_{3}^{t_{3}}f\|_{p,T}\qquad

\displaystyle\forall f\in L^{p}(T;(\omega_{1}^{t_{1}}\omega_{2}^{t_{2}}\omega_{3}^{t_{3}})^{p}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{x}}),

(100)

where
\omega_{i}
are defined in
eq.

27
.

Proof.

Let
0\leq s<k+1/p
or
(s,p)=(k+\frac{1}{2},2)
. We proceed by induction on
q
. The case
q=0
follows from
eq.

97
, so assume that
eq.

100
holds for some
q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Direct computation gives

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{k,r,q+1}^{[1]}(f)(\bm{x},z)-\mathcal{S}_{k,r,q}^{[1]}(f)(\bm{x},z)

\displaystyle\qquad=x_{1}^{q}x_{2}^{r}\frac{(-z)^{k}}{k!}\int_{T}b(\bm{y})\frac{f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})}{(x_{1}+zy_{1})^{q}(x_{2}+zy_{2})^{r}}\left(\frac{x_{1}}{x_{1}+zy_{1}}-1\right)\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle\qquad=x_{1}^{q}x_{2}^{r}\frac{(-z)^{k+1}}{k!}\int_{T}y_{1}b(\bm{y})\frac{f(\bm{x}+z\bm{y})}{(x_{1}+zy_{1})^{q+1}(x_{2}+zy_{2})^{r}}\,\mathrm{d}{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle\qquad=(k+1)\mathcal{S}_{k+1,r,q}^{[1]}[\omega_{1}b](\omega_{1}^{-1}f)(\bm{x},z),

which leads to the following identity

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{k,r,q+1}^{[1]}(f)=(k+1)\mathcal{S}_{k+1,r,q}^{[1]}[\omega_{1}b](\omega_{1}^{-1}f)+\mathcal{S}_{k,r,q}^{[1]}(f).

(101)

Consequently, there holds

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}_{k,r,q+1}^{[1]}(f)\|_{s,p,K}

\displaystyle\leq(k+1)\|\mathcal{S}_{k+1,r,q}^{[1]}[\omega_{1}b](\omega_{1}^{-1}f)\|_{s,p,K}+\|\mathcal{M}_{k,r,q}^{[1]}(f)\|_{s,p,K}.

Applying
eq.

100
with
\tau_{1}=t_{1}+1
,
\tau_{2}=t_{2}
and
\tau_{3}=t_{3}
gives

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}_{k+1,r,q}^{[1]}[\omega_{1}b](\omega_{1}^{-1}f)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,r,q,s,p}\|\omega_{1}^{\tau_{1}-1}\omega_{2}^{\tau_{2}}\omega_{3}^{\tau_{3}}f\|_{p,T}=\|\omega_{1}^{t_{1}}\omega_{2}^{t_{2}}\omega_{3}^{t_{3}}f\|_{p,T},

and so
\|\mathcal{S}_{k,r,q+1}^{[1]}(f)\|_{s,p,K}\lesssim_{b,k,r,q,s,p}\|\omega_{1}^{t_{1}}\omega_{2}^{t_{2}}\omega_{3}^{t_{3}}f\|_{p,T}
.

Appendix A
Properties of spaces with vanishing traces

In this section, we show that smooth functions with vanishing traces are dense in the space

29

Lemma 39
.
